Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: Roth Conversion - Using withholding method

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Rather than start a new topic I thought I’d ask here. Anybody ever have an experience with doing a Roth conversion with W/H outside of Fidelity but sending the replacement W/H via check and deposit slip marked conversion to a Fidelity Roth? In theory it should be fine BUT I’d like to hear from anyone who has done this. Thanks!
I thought about the check idea with Vanguard, but felt it was faster and saves the stamp to just do it from the linked checking account within the tIRA and Roth brokerage accounts. Sometimes I was able to do it on line, with no help, but this year got confused by some changes to the website, so called and they took care of it. Did it on a Friday and by Monday they had withdrawn the taxes withheld from the checking account and credited it to the Roth. I have been using the term "indirect rollover" which they seem familiar with.

Next Delhi Capitals captain after Axar Patel exit: R Ashwin sends warning to DC regarding Rishabh Pant

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Next Delhi Capitals captain after Axar Patel exit: R Ashwin sends warning to DC regarding Rishabh Pant originally appeared on Cricket News. Add Cricket News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Delhi Capitals are reportedly in talks with Chennai Super Kings over a player trade.

A cryptic social media photograph from Chennai has set the cricketing rumour mill spinning.

Ravichandran Ashwin has shared a clear view on Delhi's trade targets and captaincy choices.

Delhi Capitals may soon be hunting for a new captain, and Ravichandran Ashwin has already cautioned the franchise to be careful about how it handles Rishabh Pant. His warning comes amid reports that DC have opened trade discussions with Chennai Super Kings, and that their current skipper, Axar Patel, could be the player who changes hands.

The left-arm spinning all-rounder is reportedly at the centre of the talks, which could take him to the five-time IPL champions. It is not yet clear, however, which player Delhi would ask for in return, so the deal remains very much a matter of speculation for now.

Chennai themselves did little to cool things down. The franchise shared a photograph on its official X account that shows its own captain, Ruturaj Gaikwad, deep in a relaxed chat with Patel. The picture was taken in New Zealand once India had wrapped up a win in the second ODI on Saturday.

MORE: CSK tease fans with trade rumour as they post photograph of Ruturaj and Axar

Supporters were quick to treat the post as a hint, and talk of a possible IPL trade between the two sides gained momentum.

Delhi brought Pant back to the franchise from Lucknow Super Giants straight after the IPL 2026 season ended, which means the experienced wicketkeeper-batter is home again after two years away.

KL Rahul is also part of the squad, so DC now have two senior names who could lead the side on the field. That depth gives the management some freedom, and it is easy to see why they might feel tempted to let Patel go.

Ashwin accepts that the deal could happen, but he believes Delhi must be wary of several things if they let their captain walk out. In his view, the franchise should ask for a player such as Dewald Brevis or Noor Ahmed in return. Kuldeep Yadav has already moved to Lucknow in the deal that brought Pant back, so Delhi are short of spin options at the moment.

On his YouTube channel 'Ash ki Baat', the former India spinner started by questioning the very logic of the trade. He also pointed out what Axar's arrival would mean for the Chennai squad, as it would leave one of their overseas spinners without a role.

"Why is this trade even possible? Think about why this trade could even make sense. A player of this commodity can give them balance. If Axar comes, they will not need Akeal Hosein at all. Why is Delhi even thinking, if at all they are?" he said.

Ashwin then moved on to the players he would want Delhi to chase, and he leaned on Brevis's recent record with a Sourav Ganguly-coached side as the main argument.

"Where Ganguly was the mentor at Pretoria Capitals, Brevis did well single-handedly, and he will rate him. They might go after him. And Brevis is with CSK. They let Kuldeep go, so Noor Ahmed could be enticing too," he added.

MORE: 'Duniya Hila Denge Hum': Axar Patel trades cheeky banter with Arshdeep Singh amid Hardik Pandya PBKS-MI trade buzz

An Axar exit would leave Delhi with the big job of finding a new leader, and Pant is the name that comes to mind first because he has captained the franchise before.

Ashwin, however, does not want the left-hander to be handed the role again. He thinks veteran opener KL Rahul is a far better fit to fill the gap if the trade goes through, and instead Pant should be left free to express himself as a player, without the weight of leadership on his shoulders.

"It might be possible. It can happen. KL Rahul is a good and experienced option. Pant needs to play freely. If someone can convince him not to take captaincy and play freely as a player, it is Dada (Ganguly). No one else can. It may not be a bad call," Ashwin said.

MORE: DC coaching staff, IPL 2027: Full list of Delhi Capitals coach and support staff

From a purely cricketing point of view, Delhi should hold on to Axar Patel rather than hand him over to Chennai. Indian all-rounders who can bowl four tight overs of left-arm spin and then bat anywhere between No. 5 and No. 7 are among the rarest assets in the IPL, and that makes Axar close to irreplaceable in the way DC build their side.

The destination makes the idea even harder to defend. Chepauk has long suited spinners, so Axar would solve what is arguably Chennai's biggest tactical gap, and Delhi would be strengthening a direct rival in the process. It is difficult to see the benefit in that, however attractive the cash or the swap might look on paper.

MORE: Will Starc play IPL 2027? Exploring chances of ban and DC releasing him

Unless Chennai offer something extraordinary, such as a proven Indian middle-order powerhouse or a marquee fast bowler, the numbers simply do not add up for Delhi. Losing their captain and their most dependable utility player would also leave them hunting for both a leader and a spinner at the same time, and that is a lot to ask of any franchise in one window.

The presence of Pant and Rahul gives the management comfort on the leadership front, but comfort is not a reason to sell. Ashwin's own list of targets shows how thin the market is for a like-for-like replacement. DC would be wise to keep Axar and let the trade talk fade away.

Alexandra Daddario Officially Joins Breaking Bad-Like Crime Thriller Based On A True Story

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Alexandra Daddario has set her next role.

The popular star is known for her Emmy Award-nominated performance as Rachel Patton in HBO's The White Lotus season 1, Annabeth Chase in the Percy Jackson movies, Lisa Tragnetti in HBO's True Detective season 1, Rowan Fielding in AMC's Mayfair Witches, Summer Quinn in 2017's Baywatch, and voicing Lois Lane in the animated Justice League: Crisis on Infinite Earths films. More recently, she played Debs in the romantic comedy Couples Weekend and is portraying Kitty Hershey in the upcoming Hershey biopic.

Now, Daddario has been cast in The Long Island Bank Robber, a new crime thriller movie based on the true story of Stephen Trantel, who, after getting fired from his job as a Wall Street commodities trader, becomes one of Long Island's most prolific bank robbers. The Terminal List star Taylor Kitsch plays Stephen, while Daddario was cast as his wife, Jeanne Trantel, who gets increasingly suspicious about her husband and longs for a different kind of life. They are joined by Christopher Walken, who is playing Stephen's father, a retired police officer living by an unyielding moral code.

Matthew Gentile is the director and co-wrote the screenplay with Bianca Vera. The movie is based on the non-fiction book 'Til Heist Does Us Apart, by Jeanne Callahan and Michael Harvey, which explores Trantel's real-life story. Filming begins this month in New Jersey, and worldwide sales rights have been acquired by VanEast Pictures ahead of the movie launching the project for buyers at the upcoming American Film Market. Read statements below from Gentile and Danielle Gasher, the latter of whom is the founder and CEO of VanEast Pictures and a producer of the film:

Matthew Gentile: The Long Island Bank Robber is a true story I learned about in my youth, growing up in New York City as a true-crime obsessive. A propulsive heist film on the surface, the story also tackles complex themes of grief, survival, and marriage. With Taylor, Alexandra, and Christopher as our three leads — I could not have dreamed of a better cast to bring this film to life.

Danielle Gasher: This package is a rare find, with that perfect combination of a gripping true-crime script, a talented director, and a star-studded ensemble. We can't wait to get this one in front of buyers.

While The Long Island Bank Robber is based on a true story, it is reminiscent of Breaking Bad with a previously ordinary man becoming successful in a secret life of crime, a discerning wife who catches onto what her husband is doing and has her own ambitions, all of which is threatened by a family member who has a long history of working in law enforcement. Instead of Bryan Cranston as Walter White, Anna Gunn as Skyler White, and Dean Norris as Hank Schrader, there's Kitsch as Stephen Trantel, Daddario as Jeanne Trantel, and Walken as Stephen's father.

How A Salesman Accidentally Convinced Shaquille O'Neal To Buy 3 Ridiculously Expensive Cars

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Shaquille O'Neal doesn't do small or understated. The 7-foot-1 four-time NBA champion has an instantly recognizable brand. Oh, he's worth around $500 million, too. Naturally, as with many other well-to-do star athletes, he has a penchant for flashy cars. Problem is, he seldom fits in them without some contortions and modification. It's what drove him to have a Lamborghini lengthened into a Franken-Gallardo. That stretched supercar isn't the only time Shaq did some silly stuff with expensive cars.

In what was almost certainly a prideful purchase, O'Neal bought three outrageously expensive cars on the spot. Why? All it took was one condescending question from a less-than-respectful Rolls-Royce dealership salesman, and Shaq bought the lot. Just like that, the champ and business mogul had three extra opulent cars he says "I never drive."

Don't believe it? One of them, a 2004 Rolls-Royce Phantom, just sold on Bring-a-Trailer. If that wasn't enough, the once-undisputed heavyweight champion of the world, Mike Tyson, may have had a hand in nudging the Diesel to buy three ultraluxe cars instead of a solitary sedan. Of course, it's not difficult to get carried away when shopping for Rolls-Royces; just look at the one-off Phantom inspired by "Goldfinger."

Some 30 years ago, Shaquille O'Neal signed a groundbreaking $120 million deal to don Los Angeles Lakers colors and put away his Orlando Magic jersey. Several years later, he wandered into a Rolls-Royce showroom with his friend Alex and started browsing the hand-built grandeur.

Now, O'Neal had money to spare, but he wasn't quite up for indiscriminate spending. "I like to spend money, but I don't like to waste money. So I don't want to spend $400,000 for a car," O'Neal recalled in a Revolt interview. He went about asking a salesperson about the prices of the showroom's many Roll-Royces. That's when the salesperson said the wrong thing. Or the right thing, considering the commission on three hilariously expensive cars.

In O'Neal's book, "Shaq Uncut: My Story,"  he recalls the question as especially disrespectful, be it accidental or deliberate. The salesperson gave the Lakers sweatpants-clad O'Neal a glance and, per Basketball Network, asked, "Are you sure you can afford it, sonny boy?" That did it. Shaq lost it. "I said, 'What the f*** did you just say to me?'" So, he pointed around the showroom. "I want that silver hardtop one, and I want the green one, and give that red one over there, too."

One of those cars, the silver hardtop O'Neal selected, was a 2004 Rolls-Royce Phantom. That car sold in November 2026 on Bring-a-Trailer for $62,000. Not exactly the $320,000 or so O'Neal likely paid for it when it was new in 2004. Although he was shopping in a Rolls-Royce dealer, Shaq's book mentions a handful of Bentleys as part of the splurge. Whichever British luxury marque made O'Neal's list of picks, it was quite the spendy visit to a dealership.

Shaq's financial flexing and what is likely the most impressive commission that Rolls-Royce salesperson has ever made makes for a pretty crazy story. Believe it or not, it gets even more wild from there. According to O'Neal's book, "Iron" Mike himself, Mike Tyson, was in the mood for Bentley shopping just as the Laker found himself at the dealership.

Shaq recalled seeing Tyson picking out a couple of Bentleys and the need to out-do the champion pugilist. "Next thing I know Tyson picked out two. He's buying two of these cars, so you know what I've got to do. I've got to buy three!" Though it's not clear what ratio of Rolls-Royce to Bentleys made up his impulse shopping spree.

Be it Iron Mike or the mouthy salesperson who drove Shaq to pull the trigger, the outlandishly expensive price tag for the purchase hit him after. "A million dollars gone in 10 minutes," the massive athlete once lamented in an interview with Vanity Fair. Frankly, that's not the only time O'Neal has had the rotten luck with luxury cars. After spending the money to have it customized to fit his considerable frame, his Lucid Air Coupe ended up in a salvage yard less than two years later. Bummer.

Patriots RB Rhamondre Stevenson reportedly expected to miss a 'few weeks' with tendon injury in knee

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

New England Patriots running back Rhamondre Stevenson has a plantaris tendon injury in his knee and is expected to "miss the next few weeks," NFL Network's Ian Rapoport reported Monday.

ESPN's Adam Schefter also reported the injury, but that it's expected to "sideline him for a short period of time." It's not clear from the reports what Stevenson's timeline actually is, but it sounds like he could miss multiple games.

Details about the nature of the injury and how he sustained it are also sparse, but it leaves an already shorthanded Patriots backfield even thinner.

TreVeyon Henderson is sidelined indefintely with what he announced last week was "an unexpected and unusual medical emergency involving a series of unexplained neurological symptoms" that required several days in the hospital. He noted that tests have "come back positive and reassuring," but it's unclear when or if he'll return to the Patriots this season.

With Stevenson and Henderson sidelined, Corey Kiner is line to take on the role of lead back in New England. The second-year back has 244 yards and one touchdown on 4.9 yards per carry this season and had overtaken Henderson in the pecking order before Henderson was sidelined.

It's not clear if the Patriots will seek to bolster the backfield in the trade market. If so, they'll have to act quick ahead of Tuesday's 4 p.m. ET trade deadline.

The Patriots are coming of a 45-27 loss to the Packers on Sunday that dropped them to 5-4. That has them in line for the final wild-card berth in the AFC as of Monday.

GOFO White Paper Highlights Structural Shift in U.S. Last-Mile Delivery

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The white paper examines changing carrier dynamics, fragmented e-commerce demand and strategies for shippers navigating an evolving parcel market.

LOS ANGELES, Nov. 9, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- GOFO, a technology-driven global last-mile delivery carrier, highlights a structural shift in the U.S. parcel market in its industry white paper, How Fragmenting E-Commerce Is Rebuilding the U.S. Last Mile, first introduced at PARCEL Forum 2026 in September. Drawing on third-party market research, industry data and customer case studies, the report examines how changing strategies among established carriers and increasingly fragmented e-commerce demand are creating room for a broader range of delivery networks and reshaping carrier selection for shippers.

According to ShipMatrix data cited in the white paper, U.S. domestic parcel volume reached 23.9 billion packages in 2025, an increase of 0.4% year over year (ShipMatrix, 2026). Amazon Logistics surpassed USPS as the nation's largest domestic parcel carrier by volume, delivering 6.7 billion packages, up 9.9%, compared with USPS's 6.6 billion (ShipMatrix, 2026).

Amazon's expansion represents a significant development in a market historically dominated by UPS, FedEx and USPS. Meanwhile, UPS has been reducing lower-value Amazon volume, FedEx is emphasizing higher-value shipment segments, and USPS continues competing for lightweight e-commerce deliveries while adjusting its pricing and service mix.

On the demand side, e-commerce is becoming increasingly fragmented. According to eMarketer estimates cited in the report, Amazon accounted for approximately 40.5% of U.S. retail e-commerce sales in 2025, with the remaining 59.5% distributed across other marketplaces, retailer websites and direct-to-consumer channels (eMarketer, 2025). This diversification is creating more varied shipment patterns, fulfillment arrangements and delivery requirements.

As traditional carrier strategies and e-commerce demand evolve, alternative delivery networks are expanding their presence in the U.S. parcel market. ShipMatrix's "Other Carriers" category, which includes retailers' own delivery networks and independent regional carriers, reached approximately 2.5 billion packages in 2025, with reported growth of 13% (ShipMatrix, 2026).

The white paper identifies a range of emerging operating models, including cross-border specialists, technology-enabled urban delivery platforms and super-regional networks. Rather than following a single standardized approach, these carriers serve different geographic markets, shipment profiles and fulfillment needs.

This evolving landscape is giving shippers a broader range of delivery options. The report recommends evaluating carriers based on total transportation costs, geographic coverage, service reliability and technology integration, while maintaining diversified carrier relationships to reduce dependence on individual networks.

Customer Case Studies Highlight the Importance of Carrier Readiness

Drawing on GOFO's experience with Dr. Squatch and ShipMonk, the white paper illustrates how alternative carriers can support shippers when existing delivery arrangements are disrupted.

According to the case studies, Dr. Squatch expanded its relationship with GOFO from a monitored pilot to a core carrier arrangement after an existing provider became unavailable at short notice. During the transition, the average calendar transit time improved from 3.75 to 3.15 days.

ShipMonk faced an unplanned carrier transition within its Economy delivery tier. GOFO became operational within ShipMonk's Virtual Carrier Network in 10 days and completed onboarding across its network nodes within 35 days. Over a comparable off-peak period, average transit time improved from 2.56 to 2.10 business days.

Both cases illustrate how advance carrier integration and diversified delivery options can support service continuity during unexpected disruptions.

The white paper concludes that U.S. last-mile delivery is undergoing a long-term structural realignment rather than a temporary market cycle. As delivery networks diversify, operational flexibility, real-time shipment visibility and technology integration are expected to become increasingly important in carrier evaluation.

For shippers, adapting to this environment will require delivery strategies that balance cost, coverage, reliability and flexibility across different fulfillment models.

The full white paper, How Fragmenting E-Commerce Is Rebuilding the U.S. Last Mile, provides additional market analysis, customer case studies and a detailed carrier evaluation framework. It is available on GOFO's website.

GOFO is a technology-driven global last-mile delivery carrier founded in Los Angeles in August 2023. Guided by the brand philosophy "Drive Efficiency, Deliver Trust", GOFO combines advanced technology with localized operations to provide reliable and scalable delivery solutions. GOFO's network spans 47 U.S. states, supported by more than 300 hubs and stations. In Canada, GOFO has established a delivery network anchored by two primary hubs in Vancouver and Toronto, with service coverage across five provinces. Supported by an integrated North American network, automated sortation, intelligent routing, and disciplined operations, GOFO provides GOFO Parcel for standard parcel delivery and GOFO Ground for heavyweight, oversized, and complex shipments, delivering efficient, reliable, and scalable last-mile solutions across diverse markets. More information is available at gofo.com.

UBS: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Proposed Swiss capital rules could constrain the bank's global expansion, even as integration progress unlocks shareholder returns.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Oct. 28, 2026, UBS (UBS +2.47%) announced that it expects total capital returns to exceed pre-acquisition levels. Completing the Credit Suisse integration ahead of schedule frees up capital for share buybacks and dividend growth. Three things management said stand out.

The bank is wrapping up system migrations ahead of its original timeline. This progress reduces operating drag and allows management to focus on core wealth management growth.

Earnings generation and synergy delivery enabled management to accelerate shareholder payouts. The firm has already repurchased $4.8 billion of shares year to date while building reserves for dividends.

Proposed Swiss capital rules could impose strict buffer requirements on the bank. Management warns that overly stringent regulations will harm shareholder value and limit global expansion.

Diversified global operations and disciplined cost controls drive profitability across international wealth markets. Solvency, measured by Common Equity Tier 1 capital, underpins this operational strength.

A broad wealth network and strong Asian presence provide structural advantages while leadership watches Swiss regulatory debates to preserve pricing discipline. Operational execution remains focused as integration concludes.

Dutch Bros vs. On: Which Stock Is a Better Buy?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Both are growth stocks, but one stands out more than the other.

Can high-growth consumer brands maintain their momentum in a shifting economy? Today we compare Dutch Bros (BROS +6.63%) and On (ONON +0.46%) to see which stock offers the best path for long-term investors.

Dutch Bros specializes in high-speed, drive-thru beverage service, focusing on speed and a unique culture. Swiss-based On has disrupted the athletic shoe market with its proprietary cushioning technology. Both companies are scaling aggressively, but they operate in distinct corners of the consumer market with different capital needs and margin profiles.

Dutch Bros operates a rapidly growing network of drive-thru beverage shops across multiple states. The company specializes in customizable drinks and partners with suppliers like Portland Bottling and Lieb Foods to produce its proprietary Rebel energy drink. By focusing on high-velocity service and a unique culture, it has carved out a significant niche among retail stocks.

In FY 2025, revenue reached nearly $1.6 billion, which was a 27.9% increase from the prior year. Net income climbed to approximately $79.8 million during the same period, showing improvement from the roughly $35.3 million reported in the previous fiscal year. The company continues to see strong demand for its cold beverage offerings and energy drinks as it expands its shop count.

On is a Swiss-based brand that has become a major player in the global performance apparel industry. In its latest annual report, filed for the period ending December 31, 2025, the company reported having 67 retail locations across the Americas, EMEA, and Asia-Pacific. It sells products globally across more than 90 countries.

During FY 2025, revenue reached nearly $3.7 billion, representing an increase of roughly 30% year over year. Net income for the period was close to $248.1 million, resulting in a net margin of approximately 6.8%. Net margin shows how much of each dollar in sales is kept as profit after all expenses are subtracted, and the company has grown sales from roughly $2.2 billion just two years prior.

Dutch Bros faces significant risks including intense competition from other coffee chains and quick-service restaurants. The company is heavily reliant on its Rebel energy drink line, which accounted for more than 20% of systemwide sales in 2025. It also deals with price volatility for key ingredients like coffee beans and dairy products, as well as potential delays in its aggressive shop expansion strategy.

On competes in a crowded market against massive brands like Nike and Adidas. Because it operates globally, the company is vulnerable to shifts in international trade policies and supply chain disruptions. Furthermore, staying relevant in the premium footwear market requires constant innovation and effective marketing to maintain brand prestige.

On appears to be the more attractively priced option because it trades at a lower forward price-to-earnings (P/E) ratio, which compares the stock price to future earnings estimates. The price-to-sales (P/S) ratio, which measures market cap against sales over the past twelve months, also shows a difference between the two companies.

Between these two companies, I would lean more toward owning Dutch Bros. It has its challenges, which range from increased ingredient prices to intense competition to execution risk in expansion. Still, I like Dutch Bros more because it can be part of everyday routines, while purchases at On may only happen once or twice a year for some customers. Also, in tighter economic times, people may trade down in apparel, but for something like a coffee, that's often still kept in a weekly budget, as it's viewed as an affordable luxury.

I also like the overall expansion opportunities for Dutch Bros, as it is in fewer than 30 states. It also has a long-term goal of reaching over 7,000 drive-thru locations.

Over the next year, analysts also seem to have a more favorable outlook on Dutch Bros. According to CNN-tracked data, the median one-year price target among the 28 analysts covering the stock is $54, representing a gain of nearly 60% as of this writing. In comparison, among the 33 analysts who cover On, the median one-year price target is $43.40, representing a 16.1% gain. Between that short-term outlook and the longer-term opportunities with more location expansion, Dutch Bros wins this battle for me.

Jack Delaney has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Dutch Bros, Nike, and On Holding. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

EDX Markets Adds Bitcoin-Backed CBTC Trading for Canton Institutions

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: EDX Markets says it has partnered with BitSafe to introduce spot trading in CBTC, a Bitcoin-backed asset built for the Canton Network. The November 5 announcement describes the token's first centralized-exchange listing and an institutional marketplace where eligible clients can trade CBTC against US dollars.

EDX Markets says it has partnered with BitSafe to introduce spot trading in CBTC, a Bitcoin-backed asset built for the Canton Network. The November 5 announcement describes the token's first centralized-exchange listing and an institutional marketplace where eligible clients can trade CBTC against US dollars. This is not a new version of native Bitcoin; it is a separate representation intended to make Bitcoin exposure usable inside Canton's financial ecosystem.

According to EDX and BitSafe, each CBTC is backed one-for-one by Bitcoin. The asset is intended to support activities such as trading, lending and transfers in a privacy-enabled institutional environment. The partners point to distributed reserve controls and Chainlink Proof of Reserve as safeguards around backing. Those features are important, but a proof-of-reserve feed is not necessarily a complete audit of liabilities, ownership or redemption processes. Institutions still need to review custody agreements, redemption rights, legal claims and the conditions under which a token can be exchanged for its backing.

An asset created for use on an institutional chain needs reliable ways for participants to enter and exit positions. A trading pair against dollars can provide a price-discovery venue and simplify access for eligible institutions. Yet listing is not the same as deep liquidity. Effective markets require active market makers, transparent execution, operational reliability and practical transfer arrangements between the exchange and the Canton network. The announcement does not establish a certain future daily volume or demonstrate that every Canton participant will automatically have access to the product.

Native BTC settles on Bitcoin's own ledger under its network rules. A Bitcoin-backed token requires additional trust in the issuer, custodian, reserve accounting and any smart contracts or permissions used to control transfers. An institution may accept those conditions to obtain functionality it cannot easily achieve with native BTC. But the distinction must remain visible in both product descriptions and risk controls. A one-for-one target does not guarantee that a token trades at parity in every market condition, especially if redemptions are delayed or access is restricted.

Canton aims to connect financial applications while giving participating organizations control over sensitive information. A Bitcoin-backed asset could make collateral or financing arrangements easier within that environment, but it must integrate with the controls required by institutions. That might mean identity verification, permissioned counterparties and restrictions unfamiliar to users of open DeFi protocols. The commercial question is whether those features make institutional Bitcoin exposure more usable without creating too many layers of counterparty risk and operational cost.

Useful measures will include verified reserves over time, the number of eligible trading participants, actual volumes, the speed of deposits and redemptions, and any institutional collateral use. Readers should avoid treating a new token listing as an increase in Bitcoin's supply. The underlying BTC remains the backing asset; CBTC creates an additional instrument through which that economic exposure can circulate, with its own terms and risks.

Primary source: EDX Markets and BitSafe company release. For additional context, see NewsBTC’s report on restrictions affecting another Bitcoin-backed token.

Tom Brady QUITS Birmingham City just three years after investing in the club - with Blues top of the Championship

📅 2026-11-09  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Published: 16:22 EST, 9 November 2026 | Updated: 21:57 EST, 9 November 2026

Tom Brady has sensationally quit Birmingham City just three years after becoming involved with the Championship club.

The Blues said in a statement, released Monday night: 'Birmingham City Football Club expresses its sincere gratitude to Tom Brady for his involvement with and unwavering support for the club over the last three years.

'It was amazing to have someone with Tom's experience and passion associated with the club, and the club looks forward to continuing on that foundation from the training ground to the Birmingham community.'

The NFL icon joined Birmingham City as a minority investor as part of a takeover spearheaded by billionaire Tom Wagner's Knighthead Capital in the summer of 2023.

Brady's presence in the English football pyramid was heralded with a five-part documentary on the new ownership's plan to revamp the Midlands club, titled: 'Built in Birmingham: Brady and the Blues'.

Within the five-part Amazon series, Brady notably lambasted Wayne Rooney for his work ethic after installing him briefly in the dugout after despatching previous head coach John Eustace.

Tom Brady (right) has quit Birmingham City just three years after his investment in the club

The NFL legend became a minority owner three years ago but has now called it quits

The fly-on-the-wall documentary showed Brady visiting the Birmingham training ground to observe Rooney's team meeting and training session. While driving away, he tells his business manager Ben Rawitz: 'I'm a little worried about our head coach's work ethic.'

Rawitz replies: 'Comes across as lackadaisical.' Rooney was sacked after 83 days in charge.

The documentary later saw Brady admit his error in replacing Eustace with the former England international and reveal that he was warned against making 'sweeping changes' so early in his time with the club.

'I had good advice, "Don’t go in there and make sweeping changes. You guys have time",' said Brady. 'But we made sweeping changes that put us in decline. That was our doing.'

Under the aegis of Brady and Knighthead Capital, Birmingham City have launched what the group see as a 20-year megaproject, with £3billion earmarked for investment in a 'Sports Quarter' in the city.

The 48-acre site in the east of the city is set to feature a new 60,000-seater stadium and training ground above a network of underground transport tunnels.

But since the plan was announced, there have been some wrinkles with the timeline – pushing the stadium's completion deadline from 2029 to 2031.

Daily Mail Sport understands that London-based architects Heatherwick Studio – in conjunction with Kansas City-based Manica – are set to work on a design with a 'chimney theme' in a nod to the city's industrial heritage.

Peaky Blinders creator and famous Bluenose Steven Knight is believed to be working with the team to ensure it is 'one of the most-talked about stadium designs ever'.

Last season, Brady had promised promotion to the Premier League. The club are top of the Championship going into the last international break of the year, though he has departed before they can reach the top flight again.

Clouds Were Once Photography’s Greatest Challenge

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Met Museum’s “Chasing Clouds” is a pleasing romp through the technical problem of capturing clouds at photography’s beginning.

The original 1996 Windows XP default background is arguably the most viewed photo of all time; its cumulus puffs drifting across a way-too-blue sky are seared into my and countless others’ Y2K childhoods. Such images of fluffy clouds are so ubiquitous that it’s hard to believe they once presented one of photography’s greatest technological challenges. The Met exhibition Chasing Clouds is a pleasing romp through the history of this problem through a union of art and science, spanning the early 1800s to 1929 — roughly photography’s first century. While the medium showed promise as “the pencil of nature,” its first processes were overly sensitive to blue light, registering the sky as a blank white space, impossibly overexposed.

While this lent some images a minimalist mystique, like John Beasley Greene’s “The Nile in Front of the Theban Hills” (1853–54), other photographers resorted to painting clouds into their landscapes by hand. An initial breakthrough came when French photographer Gustave Le Gray figured out how to stitch together two pictures — one calibrated to sky, and the other to land and sea. (Photographic fakery is as old as the medium itself.)

Though the exhibition begins with painted cloud studies and ends with photographic modernism, visual discoveries unfold in a dialogue between painting and technology rather than as a scientific march from one to the other. This makes for a much more interesting story; obsolescence is often a capitalist public relations trick.

Victor Wembanyama's Spurs teammates catching his alley-oop fever

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

PHOENIX - A feathery pass lofted toward the basket from the free-throw line followed by a perfectly timed explosion skyward for a vicious one-handed slam.

The sensational alley-oop dunk the Spurs executed late in the third quarter of their 124-109 win at Sacramento on Friday was a thing of beauty - but it came with a twist.

It was a Victor Wembanyama-to-Stephon Castle connection, not the other way around.

The Spurs entered Monday night's game against the Suns boasting the top alley-oop combination in Castle to Wembanyama with 14 such plays, but in Sacramento, it was the Wembanyama feed to the explosive third-year guard that went viral.

"It's cool," Castle said after shootaround ahead of Monday night's game against the Suns.

"To be able to have that kind of connection with the big fella, whether it's me throwing a lob or him throwing it to me and it being so seamless, it's a details thing. But I think with how talented we are, it produces highlights like that."

The play also exemplified a trend of late for the defending Western Conference champions in that it's not just Wembanyama throwing down alley-oop slams with authority.

In Saturday night's 125-103 win at Portland, Luke Kornet and Tarris Reed Jr. had slams courtesy of alley-oop feeds from Keldon Johnson.

In the 112-111 win over Dallas on Wednesday at the Frost Bank Center, Devin Vassell set up Dylan Harper for an alley-oop dunk, and Kornet got one on an assist from Julian Champagnie.

"It's fun," Wembanyama said of the Spurs' diverse alley-oop avalanche.

"We have to keep finding ways, keep experimenting," the 7-foot-4 center added. "But the lob is the best shot in the game, so if we got good positions and good passes, there's no reason that anybody shouldn't catch a lob."

"We're so talented, we have so many scorers, we have so many people that can create that when we do play the right way, when we do play with our teammates, first off it's fun and second off it looks beautiful," he said.

Spurs coach Mitch Johnson said the widespread alley-oop dunking highlight's the team's athleticism, offensive depth and unselfishness.

"We have nine guys on our team who, in my estimation, have been leading scorers, and remain (high-level) offensive players in this league," the coach said. "If we don't acknowledge and value and celebrate passing, it's going to be hard for us to have the right type of harmony on the offensive end and as a team. I know there's some mumbo and jumbo in there, but it's a real thing when you start talking about the ecosystem of a team in the locker room.

"And when we can acknowledge and value passing and understand and what that does for our team and then celebrate it so people feel a part of plays where I might have made the pass to you and you made the pass to the person making the shot, that's when you start talking about high-level talent playing that style of basketball that's really hard to guard."

Castle said all the alley-ooping also reflects team chemistry.

"It's just the way we like to play off of each other," he said. "We know how hard we are to guard when the ball's popping, with how much talent we have on the court at all times."

With so many weapons, alley-oops and lobs seem to be coming from all directions these days.

"The ability to do that makes us very unpredictable," Wembanyama said.

Of course, it all begins and ends with the 22-year-old French superstar, said De'Aaron Fox, who ranks second on the team in alley-oop feeds to Wembanyama with 11.

"Vic's probably the best roller ever to play the game, probably the best lob threat ever," Fox said. "People try to keep him away from the rim as much as possible, but when he does get behind the defense, I think everybody knows - us and them - it's hard to stop or there's nothing they can do."

Wembanyama's 40-plus dunks and 30-plus alley-oop slams leads the NBA. He entered the game against Phoenix ranked fourth in the league in scoring with a career-best-29.1 average and he finished last season averaging 25.0.

"We're doing a better job running the floor, spacing the floor, and he's just doing a better job of asserting himself in the right positions," Champagnie said of Wembanyama's increased scoring. "The intent behind how he's playing is a little different. He's going to dunk the ball a lot more. So the more and more he does that, the more and more he does his thing in space, it kind of just opens up. Teams have to either guard the three, guard the dunk."

And when it comes to dunking, especially of the alley-oop variety, so many other Spurs players are getting into the act.

This article originally published at Victor Wembanyama's Spurs teammates catching his alley-oop fever.

Lucid misses Wall Street’s earnings expectations, expands loan from Saudi Arabia fund

📅 2026-11-09  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Lucid Group missed Wall Street's third-quarter expectations as the embattled electric vehicle maker continues an "operational reset" aimed at cutting costs and narrowing losses.

The company on Monday also announced a $1.75 billion increase in a delayed draw term loan from Saudi Arabia's Public Investment Fund, the company's largest shareholder.

Here's how Lucid performed in the third quarter compared with average estimates compiled by LSEG:

Despite the top- and bottom-line misses, the company's adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization of a loss $726 million beat Wall Street's expectations of a loss of $611 million for the third quarter. Its net loss for the quarter was $830.9 million, down from a loss of $978.4 million a year earlier.

Lucid's 2025 third quarter included revenue of $336.6 million, a loss of $3.31 per share and an adjusted EBITDA loss of $717.7 million.

The quarterly results are the first since Lucid CEO Silvio Napoli, who started leading the company in June, announced a restructuring plan that included implementing several cost-cutting efforts and delaying the company's upcoming midsize vehicle from the end of this year until the second half of next.

"Our transformation is beginning to show progress. We are acting with urgency, strengthening the fundamentals and driving greater discipline, while continuing to invest in the priorities to deliver profitable long-term growth," Napoli said in a release.

The company's turnaround plan includes $1.4 billion in cash flow improvements this year as well as focusing attention on a robotaxi program with Uber and Nuro, a factory that's under construction in Saudi Arabia and the midsize vehicle.

Lucid said the capital from PIF is expected to give the company "additional financial flexibility as Lucid delivers on its turnaround program." It is expected to assist in funding "operations and key investments" into the fourth quarter of 2027, including the start of production for the midsize vehicle.

The additional funds boost Lucid's total liquidity to $3.75 billion, up from $2 billion to end the third quarter.

"We are grateful for the continued financial support from our anchor shareholder, which further energizes us to remain focused on execution and on our commitments as we build a stronger, sustainable Lucid," Napoli said.

Lucid said Monday that the company expects to communicate its midterm plan and targets early next year. The EV maker in May suspended its guidance and many targets, citing new leadership and time needed to develop a turnaround plan.

A Major Liquor Brand Is Making A Comeback After Filing For Bankruptcy

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

And its comeback comes with a few more spirits to its roster.

Earlier this year, college kids everywhere had reason to worry about losing Stoli Vodka. Back in January, creditors were pushing for the liquidation of Stoli Group's U.S. operations, putting both Stoli Vodka and Kentucky Owl bourbon at risk. Now, however, the company's future in the States is looking a whole lot brighter.

According to the company, it has completed its restructuring after filing for Chapter 11 bankruptcy in 2024. If the case had shifted to Chapter 7, a court-appointed trustee could have taken control of the affected U.S. operations and overseen their liquidation. But Stoli Group has since reached a settlement with creditors of Stoli Group (USA), allowing it to resume supplying products to the American market.

In a press release, Stoli says it already has inventory in a U.S. customs warehouse and is working with longtime distribution partners to get products into participating markets.

In addition to Stoli Vodka and elit Vodka, Stoli Group is expanding its lineup of products in the States with Moskovskaya Vodka, Rooster Rojo and KAH tequilas, and The Irishman and Writers' Tears Irish whiskeys. Bayou Rum is also getting updates to the rum itself, its packaging, and pricing, with U.S. production of flavored Stoli vodkas and Stoli Ginger Beer, a nonalcoholic mixer, also to come.

"The U.S. has always been an important market for Stoli, and we're eager to share that Stoli is back and better than ever," said Marina Troyanovskaya, chief commercial officer of Stoli Group. "Our products are already here, we have longstanding partners who know our brands, and we're focused on reconnecting with consumers across the country."

From Tickets to Hotels, Celine Dion’s Paris Residency Brought Big Business to the City

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If you purchase an independently reviewed product or service through a link on our website, Rolling Stone may receive an affiliate commission.

Celine Dion’s Paris residency has just finished its sold-out first run, and new data shows the legendary singer didn’t just bring the hits to the city — she brought big business, too.

According to Billboard, the first leg of the superstar singer’s residency, titled Celine Dion Paris, wrapped with more than $125 million in tickets sold across her 16 shows in September and October. Dion sold out every date of her residency at Plenitude Arena in Paris, which marked her return to the stage after a much-publicized battle with Stiff Person Syndrome in 2022. Over the five weeks, reps for the singer say more than 500,000 tickets were sold — more than any artist in the history of the arena.

But it wasn’t just ticket sales that saw a boon. Booking website Hotels.com says it saw a 49% increase in searches for accommodations in Paris corresponding to Dion’s tour dates, compared to the same time last year. Searches from Dion’s native Canada were up 20%, followed by searches from the U.S., which increased 15% over 2025.

Marriott, meantime, welcomed Dion fans to the city with a number of activations and special stays. While the hotel chain didn’t reveal exact numbers, they say they also saw a surge in interest for their Paris properties. “[Dion’s] residency, combined with our Marriott Bonvoy partnership, inspired many members to take an extra trip to Paris this year,” says Peggy Roe, Executive Vice President and Chief Customer Officer at Marriott International. “We had the pleasure of delighting our customers with access to their passions, and our hotels benefited, too.”

As the official hotel partner of Celine Dion Paris, Marriott Bonvoy allowed members to bid or redeem points for everything from concert tickets to Dion-themed hotel stays in the city. Marriott property Le Meridien Paris Arc de Triomphe, for example, offered a “Paris, I’m Ready” suite, inspired by Dion’s Eiffel Tower video announcing the show. The suite paid homage to the singer and the city, with Instagram-worthy photo walls, concert artwork, and even a microphone and speaker for a pre- or post-show karaoke session.

Part backstage dressing room and part Parisian sanctuary, Marriott says the room is “the ultimate pre-show retreat,” with guests able to “immerse themselves in a world of elegance, music and Parisian sophistication before stepping out into one of the city’s most exciting cultural moments.” It’s the ideal space for a girls night out (or fans night out) experience to top off your Celine-inspired stay.

You can book the “Paris, I’m Ready” suite at the Le Meridien Paris Arc de Triomphe online now with prices starting from €659. This is a limited-time activation tied to Dion’s string of shows, so we recommend booking ahead to ensure availability.

Marriott also offers rooms at two properties directly across from Plenitude Arena if you’re looking to book a place to stay when Dion returns next year. The singer has confirmed an additional 10 dates scheduled for 2027, beginning in May (find tickets here).

The Renaissance Paris La Defense is just steps from the arena, but the modern, industrial-style property offers a calming, spacious reprieve from the crowds and noise below. And if you do want to be closer to the action, the outdoor courtyard is filled with performers and buskers belting Dion songs on concert nights.

The citizenM Paris La Defense, meantime, offers a colorful and eclectic stay, whether you’re desiring work or play. Marriott’s citizenM brand is known for their art-filled spaces that encourage communal hangs and co-working. Modern touches abound like cozy nooks with plenty of outlets and lush indoor-outdoor spaces for drinks and networking. The citizenM Paris La Defense is just a few blocks away from Plenitude Arena, with metro and train lines to the city just minutes away.

Marriott had rolled out its popular 1-Point Drop moments for Dion’s first run of shows, allowing Bonvoy Members to bid on packages with just one point. Similar to say, a coveted sneaker release, the 1-Point Drop experiences were available on a first-come, first-served basis, to the first Bonvoy members to claim them after they were released online. Marriott has not yet revealed whether more 1-Point Drops will be available for Dion’s new string of concert dates.

For now, members can bid on “Marriott Bonvoy Moments” with their points for premium tickets to see the singer next year, along with stays at the hotel chain’s portfolio of properties across Paris. Current packages start at just 20,000 Bonvoy points as of this writing.

A sample package right now includes a pair of “Salon Premium” tickets to see Dion in May with hospitality access and reserved first-level seating, plus a one-night stay at Renaissance Paris Nobel Tour Eiffel Hotel, located in the city’s 16th Arrondissement near the Eiffel Tower. Similar to any auction, Bonvoy members can bid on the experiences using their points (rather than cash) and the highest bidder at the close of the auction wins the package.

Marriott Bonvoy is free to join, and the hotel loyalty program lets you earn points on Uber rides, Starbucks purchases and everyday dining, in addition to hotel stays.

As for the singer herself? Dion spent her time in Paris staying at the Le Royal Monceau – Raffles Paris in the city’s eighth arrondissement, drawing crowds of onlookers each night as she departed for her shows.

In addition to Dion, the tony hotel has welcomed A-listers from Michael Jackson to Madonna over the years. The Royal Monceau is beloved for being a peaceful sanctuary in the heart of the action without skimping on all the luxury amenities and touches you’d expect from a five-star stay. The hotel is also a hub for Paris’ art and fashion scene, with Philippe Starck-designed interiors and an in-house art gallery that’s become a destination for collectors and visitors alike.

Le Royal Monceau is set to celebrate its 100th anniversary in 2028. After closing for renovations in 2008, the property reopened under the Raffles brand in October 2010.

Dion, meantime, was recently spotted back in her adopted hometown of Las Vegas this month, attending one of Metallica’s shows at the Sphere.

“This beautiful adventure finishes tonight,” Dion said at her last performance in Paris for 2026. “But we’ll meet again next May. In the meantime, believe in yourselves and hold on to your dreams.”

American Public Education Reports Third Quarter 2026 Financial Results

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

~ Third Quarter Revenue, Net Income, EPS and Adjusted EBITDA All Exceed Guidance ~

~ Raises Full Year 2026 Guidance Across All Key Metrics ~

CHARLES TOWN, W.Va., Nov. 9, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- American Public Education, Inc. (the "Company") (Nasdaq: APEI), a company that transforms lives, advances careers and improves communities by providing online and campus-based postsecondary education to approximately 106,600 students, has reported financial and operational results for the third quarter ended September 30, 2026.

"We exceeded our guidance for third quarter revenue, net income, adjusted EBITDA and diluted earnings per share. These results reflect continued growth in Health+ driven primarily by campus and online healthcare enrollments and pricing. The third quarter was also our first partially operating as a single Higher Learning Commission-accredited institution, American Public University System, following completion of our institutional combination in early August," said Angela Selden, President and Chief Executive Officer.

Selden concluded, "With the institutional combination largely behind us, we remain focused on executing our long-range plan and accelerating our momentum in Health+ and Military+."

American Public Education, Inc. (Nasdaq: APEI), through American Public University System (the "System") empowers students through education to transform lives, advance careers, and serve their communities locally and globally. With approximately 106,600 students and over 250,0001 alumni worldwide, the System includes four academic units: American Military University (AMU), American Public University (APU), Rasmussen University (RU), and Hondros College of Nursing (HCN). The System is accredited by the Higher Learning Commission, an institutional accreditation agency recognized by the U.S. Department of Education. The System is a wholly owned subsidiary of American Public Education, Inc. (Nasdaq: APEI).

The System, including AMU, APU, RU, and HCN, is not affiliated with the U.S. Military or American University.

Which Airlines Are Dry (No Alcohol)? Can You Bring Your Own Alcohol?

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

For many people, alcohol is an aspect of traveling by air that they enjoy. I’m not just talking about first or business class travel, where a glass of champagne might be part of the fun. Airports are also one of the few places where you can have a drink at 5AM and not be judged (too hard).

Admittedly customs differ around the globe, based on culture, religion, etc. In this post, I thought it would be interesting to talk a bit about dry airlines, as there’s a bit of nuance to this.

Which airlines don’t serve alcohol onboard? Which airlines don’t serve alcohol but let you bring your own alcohol? And does all of this even matter?

Let’s start by taking a look at the airlines that are dry, meaning that they don’t serve you alcohol. Here are the airlines that come to mind:

Admittedly that list isn’t totally exhaustive, since there are a countless number of small airlines out there. However, I think this covers the biggest and most interesting airlines that don’t serve alcohol (though if I missed any major ones, please let me know).

There’s an interesting twist to this. While some airlines are dry, they’ll let you bring your own alcohol onboard, and then you can consume it (within reason). The following three international carriers come to mind:

Now, it’s important to note that this isn’t without strings attached:

When I flew Kuwait Airways several years back, I wrote about how we brought a bottle of wine onboard, and they gladly let us consume it. It’s not that I couldn’t be without alcohol, but I was curious to experience a BYOB concept on a plane, since that was novel for me.

There’s an added layer of complexity to all of this, as there are some airlines that aren’t generally dry, but are dry on certain routes. Several situations come to mind (let me know if I’m missing any):

As you can tell, in some of the above situations, the lack of alcohol presumably has little to do with religion or concerns over behavior, and more to do with controlling costs.

People have different takes as to how “bad” it is to fly on a dry airline. On the one end of the spectrum, there are people who will avoid dry airlines at all costs. On the other end of the spectrum, you have billions of people who don’t drink alcohol at all, so this is a non-issue.

Personally I’m probably somewhere in the middle:

Regarding that last point, I do have to give credit to airlines that are dry, but still put effort into their drink selection. Take Saudia, for example, which goes above and beyond in terms of its non-alcoholic drink selection in premium cabins, with everything from cappuccinos and espressos, to excellent mocktails, to non-alcoholic wine.

Airlines often reflect their local customs and religions, so it’s not surprising that many airlines don’t serve alcohol onboard. If you definitely want alcohol when you fly, avoid airlines like Saudia. If you would like a drink when you fly, the good news is that some dry airlines actually let you bring your own alcohol, within certain limitations.

While the above doesn’t cover 100% of situations, hopefully it’s a useful, basic rundown of some of the bigger airlines that are dry.

How strongly do you feel about dry airlines? Does this policy impact your airline choice?

From my experience, Air New Zealand doesn't serve alcohol on domestic flights. In fact, they barely serve anything - coffee, tea, or water and a tiny bag of chips or nuts. Service is finished in about 15 minutes.

I'm aware alcohol laws of a US state does not apply when you are over that state. Does service on the ground have to comply with the jurisdiction? If so, itvwould be easier to not serve on the ground. That way, you won't run afoul of laws in some states that, for example, require an ID to be checked before service regardless of age.

Turkish Airlines don't serve alcohol in their domestic lounges or serve it on domestic flights.

The new Saudi carrier Riyadh Air is dry too, obviously.

Skydance Floats First Earnings Report As Hollywood Mega-Studio: Sizing Up The New Empire

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Skydance’s highly anticipated Q3 earnings hit today, its first quarterly report since clinching the $81-billion acquisition of Warner Bros. Discovery on Oct. 6. The numbers show $73 billion in net income on revenue of about $7 billion for Paramount Skydance alone. Since the merger didn’t close until the current fourth quarter, most numbers won’t be consolidated with WBD until early next year.

The numbers are and a call set for 5 pm ET will be Wall Street’s first look at the powerhouse that controls two major Hollywood studios, streaming platforms and a giant linear TV portfolio. Skydance’s report did outline key targets and highlights of newly merged company: at least $6 billion of synergies within three years; reducing its net leverage ratio to 3.0x by the end of 2029 and reaching investment-grade credit metrics; and launching a bundled offering of HBO Max and Paramount+ in early 2027.

At Paramount Skydance, DTC revenue growth improved 11% year-over-year to $2.5 billion, led by qccelerating Paramount+ revenue growth of 21%. DTC profit was $225 million. Watch on Deadline

Studios revenue increased 23% to $1.5 billion, reflecting a strong quarter for secondary market licensing and content produced for third parties. Profit $129 million.

The company, led by David Ellison and co-CEO Ynon Kreiz, has said it expects to generate a combined $70 billion in annual revenue. It also launches with over $80 billion in debt. Some Wall Streeters fear the leverage may be crippling. Others are more sanguine but note little margin for error.

“They have not done a lot to lay out the strategy. How do you integrate WBD? What does this company look like?” asked Rich Greenfield of Lightshed Parters on CNBC.

Questions posed by him and others over the past month include: How do you protect the linear networks? Are executives ready to do battle as they head into challenging negotiations with distributors? How will they bring the debt down? Are they willing to sell assets? Can they creatively reduce leverage? What’s coming in terms of transaction related write-downs, restructuring and deal costs? What about NFL risk. The opt-out is 2029, but there’s technically a 2027 change in control provision the League could trigger.

Leverage and integration risks are high, said TD Cowen analyst Doug Creutz in a recent note.

“The strategic case for owning Skydance here is scale in content, and on that measure, the combination has no peer,” says a more upbeat Daniel Turnos of Stone X. “We also do not believe that they will default either, which is the biggest fear today.”

Since the deal close Oct. 6, the company has been setting its management team and announced major reshuffling of the decks with high profile appointments, new teams across the sprawling media empire, and surprising ousters – including of DC Studios’ James Gunn and Peter Safran announced earlier today.

Inside Designer Carla Fernández’s Vibrant Mexico City Home, Full of Indigenous Textiles and Art

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

When the Mexico City-based writer and curator Su Wu started working on her new design book, a theme began to emerge: She kept noticing the presence of pre-Hispanic architectural and design traditions in modern Mexican interiors.

It was from this realization that Mesoamerican Modern: Ancient Ideas in Contemporary Mexican Homes was born. The book features 16 homes across Mexico—those of artists, architects, designers, and more—all shot by photographer Luis Garvan.

Wrought iron doors designed by Carla Fernández.

“When I began this project, I thought that we would be documenting a history, like the aura surrounding the artifacts in a museum, the apparitions of the past,” Wu writes in the book’s introduction. “Instead, what I’ve found is the vibrant, living presence of Indigenous ideas in Mexico.”

“In the guest bedroom, pink silk curtains dyed by Fernández cast a glow in the room. The bedspread is from her personal collection of Mesoamerican textiles, made of undyed wool in Teotitlán del Valle, Oaxaca.”—Su Wu

One space that exemplifies those ideas is Casa Flora, the colorful brutalist Mérida home and studio of the Mexican fashion designer Carla Fernández. As a designer, Fernández seeks to preserve the textile traditions of Indigenous and Mestizo communities through collaborations with artisans, bringing ancient practices to a contemporary audience. Fittingly, the home is full of Mesoamerican art and textiles.

Fernández led the renovation of the house, which dates back more than a century and now hosts workshops for Taller Flora, her design lab, which focuses on traditional Indigenous clothing techniques. (You can also stay in the home; the designer lists it for stays on Airbnb.)

Fernández’s husband, the artist Pedro Reyes, created several elements of the space, including a concrete outdoor shower, as well as some of the tiles throughout the space. His sculptures—including a prototype of Mitla Sillas, chairs inspired by the pyramids of the Mitla archaeological site in Oaxaca—are found throughout the home.

The outdoor shower designed by Pedro Reyes.

Reflecting on Fernández’s home, Wu tells Vogue: “I was very moved when writing the book with the idea of the ‘void’ in Mesoamerican architecture, and how so many architects, going back to the writings of Teodoro González de León, described the patio as a central, defining feature of the Mesoamerican home, a space that is at once enclosed and open to the elements. And in Carla’s home, I think there are many moments that exemplify this idea of the boundary between interior and exterior being not a physical one, but rather a marker of movement across psychological states.”

“A latticed window, known in Mexico as a celosia, is a central part of Mesoamerican architecture, used for cross-ventilation and to create a permeable sense of separation and shadow.”—Su Wu

Prototypes of Reyes’s Mitla Sillas chairs.

For Wu, Casa Flora is also a prime example of an idea that chef Elena Reygadas wrote about in her foreword to the book, “which is that handmade things are meant to be lived with no matter how precious, or especially if precious.”

“An equipale chair made of bent wood by the Wixárika people in Nayarit sits in front of a backstrap-loom tapestry of the Torre Latinoamericana, the iconic skyscraper in Mexico City designed by architect Augusto H. Álvarez. Made by artisans from San Juan Cancuc, Chiapas, the tapestry echoes a collection of dresses from Carla Fernández that featured historic buildings in Mexico City.”—Su Wu

All images © Luis GarvanCasa Flora, Mérida, Yucatán (pages 244-263). Mesoamerican Modern: Ancient Ideas in Contemporary Mexican Homes by Su Wu. Published by Phaidon Press.

Monday Night Football live updates, TV channel, stream, where to watch NFL game tonight

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

With the NFL trade deadline less than 24 hours away, the Buffalo Bills and Minnesota Vikings have one more opportunity to make a statement on the field before teams turn their attention to potential roster moves.

Two conference contenders collide under the Monday night lights in what promises to be one of Week 9's most intriguing matchups.

This game features a fascinating strength-on-strength showdown. Buffalo enters the week boasting the NFL's top-ranked scoring offense, averaging 33.3 points per game, while Minnesota counters with the league's No. 1 scoring defense, surrendering just 13.3 points per contest.

Josh Allen and offensive coordinator Joe Brady will have their hands full trying to solve Brian Flores' aggressive defensive scheme. The Vikings have blitzed on 73.1 percent of opponent dropbacks this season, according to Next Gen Stats, the highest rate by any team through the first eight weeks in the last decade.

On the other side, Minnesota's 6-1 record has masked some legitimate offensive concerns. The connection between quarterback Kyler Murray and wide receiver Justin Jefferson has yet to materialize, and the offense has sputtered at times despite the Vikings' impressive start. With the trade deadline looming, speculation is growing about whether Minnesota could look to upgrade at quarterback.

The Minnesota Vikings are turning to their backup in prime time.

In fact, the Vikings are rolling with their backup quarterback tonight. Murray is dealing with a hamstring injury he suffered in the Week 9 loss to the Detroit Lions and Carson Wentz will start tonight's game, coach Kevin O'Connell confirmed Thursday, Nov. 5. The former No. 2 overall pick will lead the offense that ranks 31st in passing yards at 165.6 per game entering Week 9.

Both teams enter the week near the top of their respective conferences, making this more than just another regular-season matchup. A victory would provide a significant boost in momentum, confidence and positioning heading into the second half of the season. Will Buffalo's explosive offense prevail, or can Flores' defense deliver another statement performance?

TRADE CANDIDATES: NFL trade deadline candidates include Derrick Henry and another Jets surprise

The Week 9 "MNF" matchup between the Bills and Vikings will air on ESPN at 8:15 p.m. ET, the customary start time for Monday night games.

I Spent My First 25 Hours In World Of Warcraft: Forever Happily Wasting Time

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

World of WarcraftWorld of Warcraft: Forever

Here are some things I have done in the 25 hours I have played of World of Warcraft: Forever so far:

Sound awful? Well…maybe. When I think about how I could be spending that time finally weeding my garden, or doing all the real-life chores I keep putting off, yeah. Bit of a waste of time. Such a waste of time, in fact, that I have hardly even touched most of the new content in WoW: Forever. I did play the Skyborne in beta: their starting island is fine. A bit too modern-feeling for me, but fine. I’ve definitely encountered a few new quests mixed in here and there, and the aforementioned torch and sleeping bag missions were new as well (though I did the former in beta and the latter in Season of Discovery already). But I haven’t even touched the new dungeons. I’m months away (at my current pace) from the new raids. I’m not even a high enough level to check out the Riverlands yet. May as well be playing Classic, right?

But as I hike back and forth and up and down Azeroth, I tilt my camera around. That draw distance! I see Mt. Hyjal, misty, looming in the distance, waiting for me at max level. The sun rises and sets over plains and swamps. It filters through trees and fog. I /wave at the other travelers I meet on the road. I sit at campfires and chat with others who gather around. People buff me with their class’ spells when they see me, or resurrect me if they see my dead body so that I don’t have to struggle all the way back to it. One person yesterday finished a quest just as I was starting, and volunteered to stick around (wasting 20 more minutes of their own time) to help me finish too. Another time, the game’s layer system goofed while I was on a zeppelin and something like 200 of us all ran into Undercity at once, prompting humorous panic from the folks already inside. Today, myself and two others started an entire, lengthy conversation in our zone’s general chat while I was squeezing in some early morning fishing time, just by saying “Good morning” in hopes someone would say it back. It ended with someone telling us all silly, made-up stories about a warlock named Daniel.

Somehow, this time doesn’t feel like such a waste anymore. It’s…soothing, I think. The slowness, the friction. A lot of this was always present in WoW Classic, sure. But I think the little added touches in Foreverâthe extra quests for things like torches and sleeping bags, the campfires, the layering ensuring that my world is always packed with other peopleâare lending the experience some new magic that wasn’t there before. In the past, the slowness of Classic felt more like a product of the time we were in, because no one had streamlined MMORPGs to eliminate it yet. But Forever embraces that pace and leans into it as a design feature rather than a flaw, making those moments feel rewarding on purpose rather than on occasional accident. More often than ever, you’re put in positions where there’s nothing to do but say hello to other people and forge a brief connection, or to sit back and admire the way the new lighting plays on the ripples in the river. It’s WoW, but with mindfulness. And mindfulness is something I’ve been in desperate want of lately.

Given that, it’s been really, really hard for me to put World of Warcraft: Forever down, even in the face of another brilliant game launch happening simultaneously. In my brain, a task list has populated of things I want to do: a dozen or so quests, of course, plus that one extra quest I have to turn in on the other continent, and that sleeping bag scavenger hunt I’m in the middle of. I need to visit my class trainer back in Orgrimmar or Thunder Bluff here soon to learn some new skills, and also all the profession trainers, and also the bank. There are some dungeons I’d like to run soon. All of this backs up in my brain the same way my real-life chores list does, but with the distinguishing feature that I dread none of it. I am eager to take part in every aspect, even though over half of these will inevitably be time-consuming, even time-wasting affairs. I’ll get to level 60 eventually, but I’m in no hurry to do it. To my editors: I’m sorry I’m inevitably going to take so long with this review. But there are so many wonderful, pointless distractions between here and there, I can’t help but let myself be blissful and slightly bored for another 50 or more hours first.

Prime Video, Scanbox Entertainment Team on New ‘Hamilton’ Films and TV Series Based on Bestselling Swedish Spy Franchise (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Prime Video and Nordic distribution powerhouse Scanbox Entertainment have joined forces to develop a new slate of theatrical films and streaming series based on “Hamilton,” the bestselling Swedish espionage-thriller franchise created by author Jan Guillou.

The ambitious project, which aims to relaunch one of Scandinavia’s most enduring spy franchises for international audiences, will be produced by Swedish banner Dramanation, part of Scanbox Entertainment, alongside Germany’s Odeon Fiction, which is part of Mediawan. Related Stories Melanie Lynskey Dispels ‘Two and a Half Men’ Pay Rumors: ‘There Is This Insane Idea That I Made Millions of Dollars’ Melanie Lynskey to Play Erin Patterson in 'Mushroom Murders' Limited Series at Paramount+

The deal marks a major push to capitalize on established Nordic IP across theatrical and streaming platforms. A new actor set to take on the role of Carl Hamilton, the elite Swedish intelligence officer who has previously been portrayed on screen by Stellan Skarsgård, Peter Stormare and Mikael Persbrandt.

The films and series are currently in active development, with casting, directors and other key creative attachments expected to be announced in the coming months.

Guillou’s “Hamilton” novels have sold more than 10 million copies internationally and spawned numerous film and television adaptations over the decades. The franchise follows a U.S. Navy SEAL-trained Swedish operative navigating international espionage, covert missions and shifting geopolitical alliances.

Patrick Nebout, whose credits include “Midnight Sun,” “Whiskey on the Rocks” and the previous “Agent Hamilton” television reboot starring Jakob Oftebro, will executive produce for Dramanation alongside Britta Meyermann (“Spy City,” “Deadwind,” “The Seed”) for Odeon Fiction.

“Amid ongoing global power shifts and rising chaos, Jan Guillou’s Hamilton feels more urgent and relevant than ever. He is an enduring, complex and unique Scandinavian icon with true crossover appeal worldwide,” said Nebout.

“And it’s not just a man-with-a-gun concept, there is a lot more depth, nuances and political awareness to this character. Our vision is to bring all the nuances from Guillou’s novels to the surface, and I’m thrilled to team up with Odeon and Britta Meyermann on this project.”

“The unique theatrical and streaming partnership with Scanbox Entertainment and Prime Video paves the way for many exciting iterations as we explore the vast Hamilton universe,” Nebout added.

The new adaptations will span both feature films designed for theatrical release and television series for streaming, reflecting a strategy to build a broader screen franchise around Guillou’s extensive literary universe.

Brad Morley, head of content acquisitions, Nordics, at Prime Video, said the franchise fits the streamer’s ambitions to back locally rooted stories with global appeal.

“The Hamilton franchise is exactly the kind of bold, internationally resonant storytelling we champion at Prime Video,” said Morley. “Jan Guillou created one of Scandinavia’s most compelling heroes, and we’re thrilled to bring this iconic series to a new generation of viewers alongside Scanbox, Odeon Fiction, and Dramanation.”

For Scanbox Entertainment, which is part of European distribution group Vuelta, the project represents an opportunity to leverage its theatrical distribution business alongside Prime Video’s streaming reach.

“Hamilton has been a fixture of Nordic popular culture for decades, and at Scanbox we believe the time is right to introduce him to a new audience,” said Thor Sigurjonsson, CEO of Scanbox Entertainment.

“Teaming up with Dramanation, Odeon Fiction, and Prime Video lets us give this story the scale it deserves, in cinemas and on screen at home. Jan Guillou created a character that resonates far beyond the Nordic region, and we’re committed to doing justice to that legacy.”

Annica Bellander Rune, local marketing and acquisitions manager at Scanbox, and Torben Thorup Jørgensen, the company’s chief commercial officer, will also executive produce alongside Prime Video’s Morley.
