Nicole Avant to Receive Visionary Producer Award at Bahamas International Film Festival (Exclusive)

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The producer and former ambassador to the Bahamas will also be bestowed with the title of Honorary Patron of the Bahamas International Film Festival.

Producer and former U.S. Ambassador to the Bahamas Nicole Avant will receive the Visionary Producer Award at this year’s Bahamas International Film Festival.

It’s a full circle moment for Avant, who is also being honored with the title of honorary patron of BIFF, as the award-winning producer, best-selling author and philanthropist attended the 2009 edition of the festival while the ambassador to the Bahamas.

Avant will be honored on the opening night gala of the festival, whose main part runs Dec. 10 to 14 in Nassau, after a three-day affair featuring a screenwriters residency and select film screenings in Harbour Island. The Visionary Producer Award recognizes Avant’s distinctive body of work as a producer who brings powerful and often untold stories to the screen. Related Stories Lifestyle YES Scholars Confirms Ted Sarandos, Nicole Avant, Shaboozey and Will Arnett for Milestone Gala Movies Amber Ruffin Takes Jabs at DEI Rollback and Elon Musk During African American Film Critics Association Awards

“Nicole Avant is a powerhouse in her own right: a producer, author and diplomat who has opened doors wherever she has gone, and her contribution to the Bahamas is part of its record,” said Leslie Vanderpool, the festival’s founder and executive director, in a statement announcing the award. “This honour reflects the regard in which she is held, not only by this festival, but by the film industry around the globe and The Bahamas as a whole.”

Avant, who is married to Netflix CEO Ted Sarandos, produced the 2019 Netflix documentary The Black Godfather, chronicling the life and legacy of her father, legendary music executive Clarence Avant. The film was named one of the National Board of Review’s Top Documentaries of the Year and earned Emmy and NAACP Image Award nominations. She went on to executive produce the award-winning 2021 film Trees of Peace, the story of four women who form an unlikely bond while seeking refuge during the Rwandan genocide. And in 2024, she was a producer on Tyler Perry’s The Six Triple Eight, which told the true story of the first all-Black female battalion to serve overseas during World War II. That movie won five NAACP Image Awards and received an Academy Award nomination for best original song.

Storytelling has been at the heart of many of her endeavors. She began her career in in promotions at A&M Records before becoming vp at Interior Music Publishing, overseeing artist relations and music licensing. In 2023, she added author to her credits: her memoir, Think You’ll Be Happy, became an instant national bestseller. A children’s book is now in development.

“One of my earliest and fondest memories as the U.S. Ambassador to The Bahamas was meeting Leslie Vanderpool and everyone from the Bahamas International Film Festival,” said Avant in a statement. “My husband Ted and I were fortunate to be a part of this festival in its early years, and it has been a joy to watch it grow into a platform that gives filmmakers from The Bahamas and around the globe a place to be seen and heard. I have only the fondest memories of serving and living in The Bahamas. To return as this year’s honoree, and to celebrate producers and the power of storytelling, truly feels like a beautiful homecoming,”

With Avant’s presence at the festival acting as inspiration, BIFF will have a focus on producers, the creative forces behind the camera that shepherd stories from idea to screen. As such, BIFF will host a Producers Lab running throughout the Festival, with sessions on producing, pitching and development, giving Bahamian and international filmmakers the chance to sharpen their craft and bring their projects to market.

Hoping to Max Out Your IRA for 2026? Here's How to Get It Done

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

There's still plenty of time left if you're looking to build your retirement savings and save a little on taxes in 2026.

You can still make 2026 IRA contributions until next April, so there's plenty of time left if you'd like to put away some more cash for your future. Doing so could help you reach your retirement goals much faster, and if you're saving in a traditional IRA, it could also help reduce your 2026 tax bill.

Here's how to get started, whether you've already put some money in your IRA this year or you're just beginning to save.

You can save up to $7,500 in an IRA in 2026 if you're under 50, or $8,600 if you'll be 50 or older by the end of the year. This limit applies to all of your IRA contributions, not to each account individually.

High earners may have lower contribution limits if they're contributing to a Roth IRA. Double-check yours before you set aside any money here.

Review your 2026 IRA contributions to date and subtract the total amount from your annual contribution limit, determined in the previous step. The result is the amount you'll need to save to max out your account this year.

How you save that money is up to you. You could aim to set aside a certain amount per week, or put your year-end bonus in your IRA if you get one.

It's also fine if you don't manage to fully max out your IRA. Even if you manage to save a few hundred or a few thousand dollars, that money could still go a long way in the future, especially if it's invested for decades.

You can make 2026 contributions until the tax filing deadline in April, so you can spread this year's contributions out over several months if this is easier for you. But you should know that many IRA providers default to current-year contributions. You may need to contact your plan custodian to ensure your contribution is applied to the correct tax year.

Using January to April to make 2026 IRA contributions also means you'll be getting a late start on your 2027 contributions. If you'd like, you can just start fresh next year and focus on maxing out your IRA in 2027. By spacing them out throughout the entire year, you reduce how much you have to set aside in any given month.

Toukie Smith—Model, Actress and Robert De Niro's Ex—Dead at 74

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Toukie Smith, a model and 227 star who also shared two kids with ex Robert De Niro, died Nov. 4, her and De Niro’s children confirmed in a statement. She was 74.

A model has left the world after making a tremendous impact.

Toukie Smith, a model who was also known for her role as Eva Rawley in 227, died Nov. 4 after a “long, courageous battle” with multiple sclerosis, her and ex Robert De Niro’s children Airyn and Julian confirmed to The Hollywood Reporter. She was 74.

Smith, born Doris A. Smith, was born in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania in 1952 and made her debut in the modeling industry in the 1970s, inspired by her brother Willi Smith, a renowned fashion designer and often used her as his muse.

In 1989, she scored the role as Eva on the final seasons of the NBC sitcom that also starred Regina King, Marla Gibbs and Helen Martin. She went on to appear in Joe’s Apartment, Miami Vice and Goosed but later admitted it was hard for her to break through in Hollywood.

"Everybody has to get it right," she told the New York Times in 1992. "I am an actress first. I'm not a sometime model.”

Nevertheless, she joked, “I thought modeling was rough with rejection. Huh! Welcome to the real world of acting!”

But Smith’s life couldn’t simply be narrowed down into one career. In addition to acting as well as appearing in modeling campaigns for Chanel, Versace and Yves Saint Laurent, she also opened a restaurant, Toukie’s, in 1994. The establishment sat on West Houston in Manhattan and donated some of its profits to help those fighting AIDs in honor of her brother, who died from illness related to the disease in 1987.

“I opened this place because big parties are one thing,” she told the New York Times after the restaurant opened, “but now it's time for us to come together, to connect.”

Smith and De Niro—who began dating in 1988—welcomed Airyn and Julian in 1995 through a surrogate. The former couple split about a year later, but while they often stepped out together, they rarely shared more insight into their romance. As she told the Los Angeles Times when asked about the romance in 1990, “What’s great about a private life is it’s private.”

What Smith was most known for, however, is synonymous with her nickname, which she said her grandmother gave her from a children’s song about a determined fire engine.

As she told the Los Angeles Times of the moniker, “I’ve always had lots of energy.”

For a deeper look at De Niro’s family tree, keep reading…

Robert De Niro met his first wife, Diahnne Abbott, while making Martin Scorsese's Taxi Driver, in which she had a bit part as the ticket taker at a porn theater Travis Bickle patronizes in the gritty 1976 classic.

The couple also appeared together in Scorsese's 1977 tortured-romance drama New York, New York and his 1982 satire The King of Comedy.

The actor adopted Diahnne's daughter Drena, born Sept. 3, 1967, when they got married.

Drena's first Instagram post is a black-and-white snap of her as a child out to dinner with her parents and then-couple Jack Nicholson and Anjelica Huston, captioned "At this point all I wonder is WTF was I doing there ???"

She caught the creative bug early, though, and remained in show business as an actor and producer.

Growing up, "it definitely wasn't the typical family lifestyle," she told Everything Zoomer, "but there was a lot of warmth, fun, craziness." She described her father as "a non-judgmental, open-minded person, always accepting of what people feel their paths are."

De Niro and Abbott's son Raphael De Niro was born Nov. 9, 1976.

Raphael later recalled the family moving from Los Angeles (where his father was born) to New York (the city De Niro is most associated with) when he was about 10, telling The Real Deal it was a major "culture shock."

Raphael went into real estate, which also runs in the family. Per his website, he leads The De Niro Team at Douglas Elliman in Manhattan, and he's a partner in TriBeCa's Greenwich Hotel.

He shares three kids with ex-wife Claudine de Matos and has been married to stylist Hannah Carnes since March 2020.

Acknowledging his last name alone turned him into fodder for tabloid coverage, Raphael told The Real Deal, "I just try to keep my head down and keep my private life private."

Leandro De Niro Rodriguez, Drena's son with artist ex Carlos Rodriguez, died of a suspected overdose on July 2, 2023. He was 19.

On Aug. 11, 2023, which would have been her son's 20th birthday, Drena wrote on Instagram, "Thank you for the happiest and most profound 2 decades of my life, little angel. I hope you are at peace and your spirit is filled with joy today and always."

De Niro called his grandson's death a shock, telling People, "And I just then started thinking about all the things I could have, should have done maybe with him. I don't know if that would've made a difference. And so that's always playing through my mind. It shouldn't have happened."

De Niro and model-actress Toukie Smith started dating in 1988, but ended their romantic relationship several years before their twins Julian and Airyn were born via surrogate on Oct. 20, 1995.

The exes would be leading "separate personal and professional lives," De Niro's rep said in a statement at the time, but "look forward to sharing the parenting of the children."

A rep for Smith said, per the New York Times, that the pair "obviously still have a very strong bond."

Airyn came out as a transgender woman in April 2025. She told Them Magazine that her journey also brought her closer to her racial identity.

"I think a big part of [my transition] is the influence Black women have had on me," she said. "I think stepping into this new identity, while also being more proud of my Blackness, makes me feel closer to them in some way."

As for who the actress aspires to be like? Her mother, of course.

"Whenever I'm at a family gathering on my dad's side, people would always talk about how much they love my mom and how memorable she was and how happy my dad was when he was with her," she shared. "I want to be like that… I want to be remembered the way that people remember her."

De Niro signaled that his feelings for his child hadn't changed in any way, saying in a statement, "I loved and supported Aaron as my son and now I love and support Airyn as my daughter. I don't know what the big deal is. I love all my children."

Julian is a stage and screen actor who appeared on Broadway in the Tony-nominated The Sign in Sidney Brustein's Window. In 2022 he played a young Barack Obama in the Showtime limited series The First Lady and appeared on the Netflix series Zero Day, starring his dad as a former POTUS investigating a deadly cyberattack.

"For my kids, I tell them, 'If you want to be an actor or you want to do this or that, that's fine as long as you're happy. Just don't sell yourself short,'" De Niro told People in January 2020. "That's the most I would say—push yourself a little more and reach for what you really think it is you want to do. Don't be afraid."

De Niro married Grace Hightower on June 17, 1997.

The welcomed son Elliot De Niro on March 18, 1998. De Niro filed for divorce from the Precious actress in August 1999, but they reconciled and renewed their vows in 2004.

“They can make sure this one sticks," the actor joked of the two judges who officiated, per People.

In 2009, De Niro, then 66, told the Los Angeles Times, "My whole thing now is just to have the strength to keep my kids in line and get them to the point where they’re independent and self-reliant. I want to make sure my kids know I’m there, and you’ve got to have the stamina to do that.”

Daughter Helen Grace De Niro was born via surrogate on Dec. 23, 2011.

De Niro and Hightower split up for good in 2018, the Oscar winner saying in a statement, "Grace and I have two beautiful children together. We are entering a period of transition in our relationship which is a difficult but constructive process. I honor Grace as a wonderful mother and ask for privacy and respect from all as we proceed to develop our roles as partners in parenting."

When promoting Silver Linings Playbook, in which he played a father whose grown son (played by Bradley Cooper) has bipolar disorder, De Niro opened up about having a child with special needs.

"I don't like to get emotional," he told Katie Couric in 2013, "but I know exactly what [my character] goes through."

He shared in 2016 that Elliot has autism.

"She's so adorable," De Niro said of his then 1-year-old daughter in 2013. He told CBS News, "You just look at her, and it's all there...Nothing else matters."

Helen made a rare red carpet appearance with her dad and his girlfriend Tiffany Chen at the 2025 Cannes Film Festival.

De Niro was first photographed with Chen in August 2021, but his relationship with the tai chi expert wasn't the subject of much notice until after their daughter Gia Virginia Chen-De Niro was born on April 6, 2023.

"This baby was planned," Gayle King relayed in announcing Gia's arrival a month later on CBS Mornings, and "brought here by love."

While De Niro admittedly doesn't do the "heavy lifting," he waxed rhapsodic about having a baby in the house.

"Everything that I'm consumed with or worried about just goes away when I look at her," he told AARP in January 2024. "It's wondrous."

And at 2 years old, Gia continued to be "pure joy," De Niro told Entertainment Tonight in June 2025. "It's always wonderful."

CNN Sees Some Staff Buyouts Under Skydance Merger Plan

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A group of CNN veterans — among them camera operators and fact-checkers — have opted to take voluntary buyouts as part of an offer spurred by Skydance‘s acquisition of Warner Bros. Discovery, according to four people familiar with the matter.

The deadline for these employees to accept the buyout came Sunday, according to these people. Skydance and CNN declined to make executives available for comment. Related Stories Oscar Predictions: Animated Feature — A New and Competitive Era for the Medium That Isn't Just for Kids

Skydance offered voluntary buyouts across the company once it completed its months-long quest to purchase Warner. The company has $80 billion in debt and has pledged to find $6 billion in operational savings over the course of three years. The buyout offer varies according to the business unit in which it is offered, one of these people said. At CNN, according to two of these people, buyouts were offered to people who were in vice president roles or levels below and had more than 15 years of service.

CBS News staffers are also mulling buyout offers, according to three people with knowledge of the matter, but some of their acceptance deadlines are staggered.

The number of people accepting buyouts at CNN isn’t believed to be excessive, but there are some staffing units where more people took them. There are a number of longtime camera operators at CNN who have been with the company for three decades or more, and who may be their 50s or 60s. There are also some veterans who work in a group that works in a central news unit that reviews scripts and stories ahead of them being put on air.

CNN staffers have not had a similar buyout offer in about seven years, according to one of these people, which means a greater number of employees may be interested in such an opportunity.

Skydance, which is slated to report its first quarter of earnings following its merger, is no doubt being scrutinized to see how it manages staff reductions, says Mike Proulx, vice president and research director at Forrester Research. “The newly merged Skydance comes with massive debt that remains at the center of concern over the company’s ability to profitably grow,” he said. “How will the company reach its cost-synergy target without weakening content quality, theatrical output, and journalistic integrity, while at the same time investing in future innovation?”

Go Inside Miu Miu’s Handbag Atelier In Tuscany

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Twenty years in, the brand’s matelassé leather remains a luxury calling card.

March 4, 2006, was perhaps like any other day in the mid-aughts. Myspace was the top website in the U.S., while Beyoncé’s “Check On It” was No. 1 on the Billboard charts. Cult-classic teen mermaid drama Aquamarine had just premiered the night before. And in Paris, during the fall/winter fashion shows, the date would prove to be the unexpected start of a new era—not just for one brand, but for fashion as a whole.

At the 18th-century Left Bank restaurant Lapérouse, Miu Miu presented its first-ever show in the French capital—now its permanent runway home—signaling a crucial differentiation from its sister brand Prada in Milan. Paper-thin knits, ultra-mini dresses, peplum jackets, chunky platform wedges, and mismatched-lace oxfords walked the intimate runway, on models adorned with white eyelashes and poppy-red lips. On the handbag front, an experimental leather-making technique was introduced, one that would become Miu Miu’s signature and a staple of aughts aesthetics: matelassé.

Originating in early 18th-century Marseille, the quilting technique was traditionally used in bedding or upholstery. In Miu Miu’s collection, it was developed in the form of a leather handbag—the Coffer—a slouchy shoulder tote in chocolate brown with a twisted handle and a bold metal clasp. A counterpoint to the youthfully eccentric clothes, the bag itself was a subtle debut, with only three shown on the runway and in only one colorway. Nonetheless, it was an immediate hit, carried by Anne Hathaway, Lindsay Lohan, and Isla Fisher in its early years. For the relatively young but increasingly iconic brand, a calling card was born.

Twenty years since that collection, Miu Miu has transformed its matelassé technique into a rainbow of colorways and style offerings. The seemingly seamless Wander and boxy Arcadie styles boast prime spots in the brand’s portfolio of leather goods, especially when it comes to the matelassé iterations—just take a look at the brand’s 2026 campaign starring Gigi Hadid.

The handbags come to life in Miu Miu’s leather goods workshop in Tuscany, the architecturally award-winning headquarters called The Garden Factory, built in the Valvigna region near where Miuccia Prada’s husband Patrizio Bertelli grew up, and designed by Guido Canali in the mid-1990s. Completed in 2015, the building itself is a physical manifestation of the Prada Group’s minimalist aesthetic.

The Garden Factory’s nickname derives from the property’s one-to-one ratio of building to greenery, a complete balance of cool steel and native vines, and plenty of glass windows to bridge the two. Inside is row upon row of accessories—66,000 leather goods and 77,000 pairs of shoes—carefully filed from the collections over the years to serve as both record and inspiration. Instead of season or collection, the cabinets are organized by product category, and matelassé has its own section.

Despite its creation 20 years ago—or perhaps in part because of it—the very tactility that makes Miu Miu’s matelassé leather so appealing is hitting a peak in current luxury collections across the board. To a consumer, the in-person experience was always a factor, but in the Instagram age, brands are turning to hyper-visual signifiers of craftsmanship to keep up with the market. Be it ASMR-inducing sequins, fluffy feathers, slick latex, or impossibly buttery leather, touch and feel appeal, even through an iPhone screen, is increasingly important. To see a matelassé handbag is to wonder how it feels, and to feel one is to experience its pillowy, permanent spring, an effect somewhere between plissé and origami. That effect is intentional, yet the product itself is softer in practice. That, too, is intentional.

The quilted technique is achieved by layering elastic Lycra with thin padding and nappa lambskin leather, sourced from Miu Miu’s tannery in France. Strategic patterns are stitched onto the layers and then the Lycra is removed, allowing the leather and padding to fold back into itself without losing the valleys created by the stitches. Thus, the matelassé effect comes to life. The smaller the product—like a card holder—the harder it is to craft. 3-D modeling is never used. Leather products are always assembled by hand, an important standard for the brand, and inspected manually by experts who know every detail of the bag’s design.

How does one become an expert in Miu Miu bags, you may ask (besides simply being a loyal fan)? Prada Group has its own leather goods academy where craftspeople spend six months learning about the brands’ luxury practices and standards. Even after those six months, it takes five to 10 years for a craftsperson to know how to make an entire bag themselves, start to finish.

From anywhere in the building, you have a view of the gardens outside, along with 45-degree-angled ceilings that let light in without creating too much heat. Stations are bustling with artisans, most of them women, in teams of two. They wear white lab coats emblazoned with the Prada Group logo.

The building’s minimalism, it seems, isn’t a lack, but an open space for creation to take place. And like the windows bridging steel to vine, or the archives linking past and present, or even the stitched valleys of a matelassé handbag, Miu Miu has mastered the spaces in between.

DC Studios’ Justice League Plans & Wonder Woman Intro Reportedly Confirmed

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The DC Universe's major plans are reportedly revealed.

James Gunn and Peter Safran announced the DCU Chapter One back in January 2023, charting the course for the shared universe in animation, live-action movies, and TV shows. However, in a shocking turn of events, the duo is now out, with DC Studios set to be run by new President and Chief Creative Officer Jeremy Latcham, who was in the superhero business before as a Marvel Studios executive, working on movies like Iron Man, Avengers: Age of Ultron, and even Gunn's Guardians of the Galaxy. As Gunn prepares to leave the DCU, it seems like some of his most secret plans are coming out.

On X, The Wall Street Journal's Ben Fritz has reported that Wonder Woman will make her DCU debut in Gunn's Man of Tomorrow, with Batman's The Brave and the Bold movie planned for 2028, and Justice League plans on the way.

According to Fritz, Man of Tomorrow, which is set to fly into theaters on July 9, 2027, was planned as the first of multiple Superman sequels to be helmed by the former DC Studios creative chief. Gunn and Safran were said to be looking at another two Superman sequels, which would have evolved into full-on Justice League movies, or similar team-up films for releases in 2029 and 2031, the same two-year gap between 2025's Superman and Gunn's Man of Tomorrow. The Brave and the Bold's tentative 2028 release date is surprising, as Robert Pattinson's The Batman - Part II is coming out on February 18, 2028, following multiple delays.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Uber Wants You To Drive Yourself—Reserve Parking In 15 Cities, Earn 10% Back

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Uber is launching parking reservations in 15 U.S. cities, letting you find a garage or lot, compare prices, and pay inside its app. Uber One members earn 10% back in credits on eligible bookings and of course Uber One comes with Amex Platinum. It’s a little weird to have self-service Uber. You supply the car. You do the driving. But they offer Uber Eats for pickup, so I guess? Still, even if it’s not gig workers and urban mobility, if the price and rebate are good, ok then. Where Uber Parking Is Available Uber’s November 9 launch covers Atlanta, Austin, Boston, Chicago, Dallas, Denver, Los Angeles, Miami, Nashville, New York City, Philadelphia, San Diego, San Francisco, Seattle, and Washington, D.C. They plan to expand across the United States. This follows Uber’s acquisition of SpotHero, announced in February. SpotHero’s broader network has more than 13,000 garages, lots, and valet locations across over 400 cities in the U.S. and Canada. However, Uber’s parking page lists 7,000 facilities. Tap Parking in the Uber app, enter your destination and parking times, and compare the map’s prices. You can filter for self-parking or valet, garages or surface lots, and things like on-site staff. Enter your vehicle details and license plate, pay via your Uber Wallet, and follow the entry instructions on the parking pass. Uber already has your account and payment information. SpotHero had parking reservations. Now it’s in one place with Uber rebates. 10% Rebate, Including Taxes And Fees Uber One members earn 10% credits on eligible completed parking reservations including the parking charge, taxes, and fees. Credits generally arrive within 24 hours after the reservation. Not every location or reservation qualifies. Uber One costs $9.99 a month or $96 a year. Buying an annual membership solely for parking requires $960 in eligible annual parking purchases just to earn $96 in credits, assuming you use those credits at full value. So this is for someone who already has Uber One. Can You Use Amex Uber Cash? And Does Amazon Help? Uber Cash works at supported parking locations. Uber’s parking help page says so, and Uber’s American Express terms say: The Amex Benefit may be applied to any services to which Uber permits Uber Cash to be applied. So this is a new way to use monthly Uber credits ($15 monthly, plus another $20 in December). Consumer Gold offers $10 monthly. And you can get Uber One via Amex Platinum: up to $120 in statement credits per calendar year for membership fees charged directly to the card. Both monthly and annual plans qualify. (Consumer Centurion cards have a membership credit as well.) Keep Uber Cash off for the membership payment so Amex is charged and can reimburse it. Amazon Prime doesn’t unlock this. The new U.S. Amazon arrangement provides $0 delivery fees on eligible Uber Eats restaurant orders of $25 or more placed through Amazon, but doesn’t give you an Uber One membership. Those orders use a separate account and Uber One benefits don’t apply to them. Uber Isn’t The Only Option In This Market There are plenty of alternatives. Alternative   What To Compare SpotHero directly   The same underlying parking business, with a broader geographic footprint and hourly, monthly, event, and airport parking. ParkWhiz and BestParking   Other booking channels for prepaid garage and lot reservations, including event parking. ParkMobile and PayByPhone   Useful for paying for and extending parking in participating locations. ParkMobile also offers advance reservations. AirportParkingReservations.com and Way.com   Airport parking comparisons, including off-airport lots and hotel parking. Compare shuttle schedules, operating hours, and cancellation rules. The garage, hotel, event venue, or airport directly   Prepaid offers, parking packages, validation, and loyalty discounts that might beat an intermediary’s price. I’ve written about the economics of empty parking spaces: the garage already exists, and an unsold hour is revenue the operator never gets back. Advance booking can give operators a way to discount to people who compare prices while charging more to drivers who just show up. Uber says many participating locations offer rates below their drive-up prices. Read The Parking Pass Before You Pay Uber’s parking rules leave several decisions to the individual facility: Cancellation. Uber says most options can be canceled free before the start time, but the deadline shown for your reservation controls. Extensions. Most active reservations can be extended if space is available but don’t assume you can stay indefinitely at the original rate. Entry and exit. Follow the pass’s barcode, license plate, or attendant instructions. Most participating facilities don’t allow repeated entry and exit during one reservation. Vehicle restrictions and extra charges. Check height limits and oversized vehicle charges, along with early entry and late exit rules. Uber’s charge guidance flags additional fees. A reservation doesn’t imply a particular space is being held. SpotHero says it manages inventory with operators rather than physically holding a specific stall. If you arrive and can’t enter or the lot is full, Uber directs you to the parking pass’s help option. Good luck. There are two other launch limitations in Uber’s FAQ: business profiles aren’t supported, and Uber and SpotHero accounts can’t be linked. An Uber reservation won’t appear in your SpotHero account. The claim in the launch pitch that Americans spend an average of 17 hours a year looking for parking comes from a July 2017 study. It seems to me there’s very high variance in this. In fact, isn’t one solution just to Uber? Uber gets a transaction from people who choose to drive, then offers credits that encourage them to come back for a ride or food order. They also say they’ll explore using parking locations to stage autonomous vehicles between trips. They’re going to be losing their Waymo partnership so I wonder what that looks like? What I’d Really Like Is Uber Valet About a decade ago, Luxe offered a valet would meet you at your destination, take your car away to park it, and bring it back when you were ready. Zirx did, too. Walking into a restaurant while somebody dealt with your car was super value add! But it’s a tough business. Luxe ended its consumer valet service in 2017, and Volvo bought its technology, team, and other assets that September. Zirx stopped consumer valet service in February 2016. Attendants had to reach customers, park their cars, and return them, on top of paying for parking and insurance. Uber considered buying Luxe’s assets and engineers before Volvo did. Valet Network in New York advertises $10 parking, using street spaces. Apparently the service is in Carroll Gardens, Cobble Hill, and the Lower East Side and it’s another $3 to return the car. Ector offers something like this at French airports and train stations. That seems like an Uber feature, this is more like selling hotels through the app because they already have customers and their customers travel. I liked the old Travis vision for the company so much better. They had some growing up to do and maybe they wouldn’t have achieved everything before the VC cash ran out but this just isn’t that exciting of a vision, even if you have something new to do with existing Uber credits. Topics on this pageParkingSpotHeroUberAmazon PrimeAmazon.comAmerican ExpressAmericansAtlantaAustinBostonCanadaChicagoDenverLos AngelesMiamiNashvilleNew YorkPhiladelphiaSan DiegoSan FranciscoSeattleUber EatsWashingtonWaymo+21 more More From View from the Wing

AirportParkingReservations.com and Way.com   Airport parking comparisons, including off-airport lots and hotel parking. Compare shuttle schedules, operating hours, and cancellation rules. The garage, hotel, event venue, or airport directly   Prepaid offers, parking packages, validation, and loyalty discounts that might beat an intermediary’s price. PepsiCo to cut about 1,000 jobs as North American business struggles

📅 2026-11-09  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

PepsiCo is cutting about 1,000 jobs across North America as the company tries to turn around lagging sales in its home market.

"We have a clear ambition for PepsiCo North America: to build a stronger, more future-fit business that delivers industry-leading growth," Ram Krishnan, CEO of PepsiCo North America, wrote in a Monday letter to employees obtained by CNBC. "To do that, we need to be faster, more connected, and more capable, while continuing to invest in growth – behind our people, capabilities and brands."

Over the next week, Pepsi plans to cut jobs as it says it aims to streamline its operations and better connect teams across the company's North American business. PepsiCo employs about 306,000 people worldwide, including roughly 125,000 in the United States, as of Dec. 27, according to a company filing.

The Wall Street Journal first reported the coming layoffs.

In October, PepsiCo cut its full-year earnings outlook, citing the struggles in its home market. During the third quarter, its North American beverage unit saw volume shrink 2%, while its North American food division reported flat volume.

While the company has tried to boost sales by cutting prices and investing in marketing, the turnaround is taking longer than expected, CFO Steve Schmitt said on the company's earnings conference call.

Shares of Pepsi rose slightly in afternoon trading. The stock is down nearly 1% this year, lagging behind the S&P 500's gains of nearly 11% over the same period.

Sources: Coventry ‘doing everything’ to keep Jack Rudoni as Everton ‘ready to pounce’

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Transfer Correspondent AUTHORITY Former Sky Sports and ESPN FC correspondent who specialises in global recruitment, contract negotiations, and high-velocity transfer breaking news. FOCUS Exclusive reporting, domestic and international transfer markets, and elite-level insights. THE INSIGHT Pete utilises a Global Network of Industry Sources to deliver verified, first-to-market reporting. His “Deep-Lens” analysis connects the dots between boardroom strategy and pitch-level recruitment, providing a level of transfer clarity that goes beyond surface-level rumours.

Everton are “ready to pounce” on any chance to sign Coventry City midfielder Jack Rudoni next summer, sources have told Football Insider.

The Toffees are huge admirers of the 25-year-old, who has also attracted significant interest from Leeds United in recent weeks.

Coventry are determined to avoid selling their prized asset in the middle of the campaign, and are “doing everything” they can to keep hold of him at the moment.

However, sources say that they will find it difficult to keep Rudoni if they are relegated back to the Championship, with Everton ready and waiting for a deal.

The former Huddersfield man is only under contract at the CBS Arena until the summer of 2028, meaning Coventry lack long-term security over his future.

Rudoni starred in their promotion-winning campaign last term, and already has three goals from his opening 10 games in the Premier League.

Rudoni has been building a growing reputation for himself in the Championship in recent years, but that has hit a new level altogether since Coventry’s promotion.

The midfielder notched 35 goal involvements across his last two campaigns in the second tier, and has firmly established himself as the first name on the teamsheet for Sky Blues boss Frank Lampard.

He is one of Coventry’s main hopes of securing Premier League survival, and his clear importance to their project has only attracted further admirers from elsewhere.

Everton boss David Moyes is a huge fan of Rudoni, and feels that he could be a perfect fit in the middle of the park for his team.

However, Coventry have been firm in their stance so far that Rudoni is not for sale, although they are likely to have that stance tested moving forward.

Football Insider revealed last week that Everton are ahead of Sunderland and Coventry in the race to sign BK Hacken holding midfielder Abdoulaye Doumbia.

The 19-year-old has enjoyed a hugely impressive season in the Swedish Allsvenskan, and is now increasingly likely to earn a big move this summer.

Scottish giants Celtic are also interested in the starlet, but sources say that Doubmia would prefer a move to the Premier League.

Everton are understood to be at the front of the queue in that regard, given thir security as a top-flight club.

Musk says many Cybertruck buyers will get deposits back as production ends, arguing ‘opportunity cost’ is vastly outweighed by new robotaxi

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

After sidelining the Cybertruck as he bets Tesla’s future on the autonomous Cybercab, Elon Musk now says customers still waiting for their electric truck may not get one after all.

In an early Monday post on X, he said the number of Cybertrucks ordered “far exceeds our production rate,” which means many buyers will be out of luck following the CEO’s announcement Friday that Tesla would be “winding down” the electric truck’s production by the middle of next year.

“We will try to make as many as possible before shutting down the line in about 8 months, but will have to return deposits for a lot of people,” Musk said Monday.

The news comes as some fans of the boxy and futuristic-looking Cybertruck said on social media that they were putting in new orders or upgrading their existing Cybertruck orders before the vehicle ends production. As of Monday, Tesla’s website seemed to still be accepting new orders.

Musk said in his latest post that Tesla was shutting down Cybertruck production not because the vehicle was unprofitable but because, “the opportunity cost of the complexity of Cybertruck is vastly outweighed by the value of incremental Cybercab production.”

The Cybertruck, unlike other Tesla vehicles like Model Y, has struggled to reach and maintain high sales since it was released in 2023. When the vehicle was first announced in 2019, Musk hailed it as Tesla’s best product “ever.” He later predicted Tesla would make between 250,000 and half-a-million units of the vehicle per year. Yet, between 2024 and 2025, Cybertruck sales plummeted 48% to 20,237 from 38,965.

Through the third quarter of this year, Tesla had sold an estimated 11,586, according to Kelley Blue Book. Meanwhile, Tesla sold 262,491 Model Ys and 106,962 Model 3s over that same time.

In recent years, Tesla has discontinued other models like the Model S luxury sedan and Model X SUV, narrowing its consumer vehicle lineup as Musk increasingly shifts the company’s focus to autonomous vehicles, but also humanoid robots. Tesla’s focus could drift further away from cars as it explores a merger with Musk’s other public company, SpaceXAI.

To be sure, the Cybertruck has still amassed a cult following for its unique design despite, or sometimes because of, the association with Musk, whose rightward shift once made him a close adviser of President Trump and the leader of the Department of Government Efficiency (DOGE).

After a brief falling out with Trump, he returned to the fold and reportedly spent as much as $474 million in the midterm election cycle to boost Republican candidates. But many of his preferred candidates lost, following a surprisingly strong showing for the Democratic Party in the midterms last week.

The end of the Cybertruck’s production run also triggered criticism from Tesla fans who accused the company of neglecting existing customers as Musk chases a future where consumers don’t own their own vehicles.

“Watching the decisions Tesla is making recently has just left me puzzled as a consumer. We went from having lots of choices in vehicles. To those being whittled down,” wrote one X user.

When Tesla unveiled the Cybercab concept in 2024, Musk portrayed the vehicle as “a little lounge” rather than a car in the traditional sense. He pitched a future of “individualized mass transit,” where people wouldn’t necessarily need to own a car, but could buy one if they wanted.

He noted that ownership can be expensive, between the price of the car, insurance, and storage. But the price per ride in a Cybercab, he predicted, would be about 30 to 40 cents per mile.

“Your average passenger car is only used about 10 hours a week out of a 168 hours. So the vast majority of the time, cars are just doing nothing. But if they’re autonomous, they could be used, I don’t know, 5 x more?” Musk said at the time.

In reply to Musk’s post Monday, X users pushed back on this vision.

“The problem is no one is going to want to give up their personal cars for a ride share service. Are you going to annihilate uber? Yup. Are you going to be every Americans daily transport via Robotaxi anywhere in the near future? Nope,” the user wrote.

Other X users complained that the company was cutting out customers whose needs aren’t met by other vehicles in Tesla’s lineup, while others said Tesla should be expanding vehicle production not cutting it.

“This is still not making any sense. Why even make the Cybertruck in the first place?,” wrote one user.

Role: Reporter Marco Quiroz-Gutierrez is a reporter for Fortune covering general business news.

JetBlue Launches 3 New Nonstop Routes In Only 1 Day: See Full List Now

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

New routes are usually intriguing, and can be especially notable when more than one launch on the same day. That is what happened with JetBlue Airways last week, as the carrier introduced two links from Fort Lauderdale-Hollywood International Airport (FLL) and one from Baltimore/Washington International Thurgood Marshall Airport (BWI).

JetBlue's growth at FLL has been significant recently, as the airline benefits from the end of the now-defunct Spirit Airlines. In the present week, Cirium Diio data indicates that FLL is currently the airline's most-served airport. 856 weekly departures are available, exceeding those from New York JFK (816) and Boston (811).

On November 2, the carrier took off from BWI to San Juan's Luis Muñoz Marín International Airport (SJU). As Puerto Rico is a US territory, flights are considered domestic. As the service only covers 1,360 nautical miles (2,519 km) on a great circle basis, it is only JetBlue's ninth-longest route from the Lower 48 states to SJU through next summer.

Flown daily on a year-round basis, it is the first time that JetBlue has served the airport pair. The airline will be one of three carriers running nonstop, joining Frontier Airlines (up to one daily service) and Southwest Airlines (up to three daily flights). JetBlue's entry follows the exit of Spirit earlier this year.

Given the schedule, the operating Airbus A320 will fly from somewhere to SJU to BWI to SJU and then elsewhere. For example, on November 8, Flightradar24 indicates that the airframe, the 20.5-year-old N638JB, flew from Hartford to Puerto Rico. It then flew to Maryland and back, remained overnight in SJU, and flew to FLL the next morning.

Also on Monday, November 2, the airline began flying from FLL to John Glenn Columbus International Airport (CMH). Flights run daily throughout the year. While the 162-seat A320 is nearly always scheduled, the 140-seat A220-300 is due to appear from September 8, 2027. As that is so far away, it is quite possible that it'll change. The operating airframe remains overnight in Ohio.

JetBlue was interested in this market because Spirit pulled out early this year and had up to two daily services. It joins Frontier, which entered the market in March this year (up to a daily departure), and Southwest (usually daily, but up to four daily services for spring break). JetBlue's entry means that the airport pair has up to six daily flights, just like it did when Spirit operated, although that frequency is very time-limited.

In the 12 months to July 2026, the US Department of Transportation shows that 235,200 round-trip passengers flew between FLL and CMH. Excluding seasonality, around 640 people traveled daily. About three-quarters of the traffic flew nonstop, meaning approximately 57,000 passengers (156 daily) flew via a hub or other airport en route. Spirit had 38% of the total traffic (89,500).

They're from five US airports. Find them all here!

Launched on (you guessed it) November 2, FLL to Indianapolis International Airport (IND) follows the same pattern as the other two routes. But it is even more significant because it is the first time that the carrier has flown to IND and Indiana.

Spirit operated FLL-IND earlier this year, which motivated JetBlue's entry. Like the other routes mentioned earlier, it is also primarily served daily on the A320. However, the A220 (shown above) is scheduled from December 17, 2026, until January 3, 2027, then from February 11 to 22, and again from September 8 next year.

JetBlue is one of four operators running nonstop between FLL and IND, coexisting with Allegiant Air (up to 12 weekly), Frontier (up to one daily), and Southwest (up to three daily departures). When added to CMH and the upcoming transcontinental route from FLL to San Diego, JetBlue has 62 routes from FLL this winter.

When you can spread the flu and when to return to work: 12 facts

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

These 12 facts explain how long the flu is contagious, tracing when influenza spreads from the day before symptoms to the CDC's fever-free threshold for work and school

The flu can spread before anyone knows they have it. An adult infected with influenza can pass the virus to others about one day before the first fever, ache or cough appears. The same person can keep spreading it after the worst of the illness has passed and they feel ready to get back to normal life. Contagiousness and feeling sick do not follow the same schedule, and that mismatch is what makes the question hard to answer.

So how long is the flu contagious? The answer depends on where a person is in the illness and who that person is. The incubation period, the time between exposure and first symptoms, usually runs one to four days, with an average of about two days. Contagiousness tends to peak in the first three to four days of illness. Most healthy adults can infect others for roughly five to seven days after symptoms start. Children and people with weakened immune systems can shed the virus for longer.

These timelines carry practical weight. They shape when a parent keeps a child home from school and when an employee returns to the office. They also shape when a household can stop taking extra precautions around a vulnerable relative. A person who goes back to work as soon as they feel better may still be carrying enough virus to pass it on. Fever-reducing medicine such as acetaminophen or ibuprofen can also hide a fever that has not truly broken.

The 12 slides that follow trace the flu from first exposure to recovery. They cover the incubation period, the day of spread before symptoms, the peak contagious days and the longer timelines for children and immunocompromised people. They also cover the CDC's 24-hour fever-free guidance for returning to work or school. Other slides explain the main ways the virus moves between people and how antivirals such as Tamiflu fit in. The final slides look at the cough and fatigue that can linger into recovery, along with everyday steps that limit spread.

Individual timelines vary, and the ranges here describe typical patterns rather than a fixed schedule for any one person. A doctor or other clinician can give advice for a specific case. That matters most for anyone with a weakened immune system or a young child at home.

The flu incubation period, the stretch between catching the influenza virus and feeling the first symptoms, usually lasts one to four days. The average is about two days.

Measured in days rather than weeks, the gap is short. A person exposed on a Monday would typically notice symptoms somewhere between Tuesday and Friday, with Wednesday close to the average. One new case in a household or office can be followed by others within the same week.

Incubation is the silent phase of the infection. The virus has entered the body and started to multiply, but no signs of illness have appeared yet. There is no fever, no ache and no cough to signal that anything is wrong. Most people carry on with work, school and errands as usual. They have no reason to suspect they are infected.

The moment of exposure is often hard to identify. Influenza passes between people during ordinary close contact, and those encounters rarely stand out. A shared elevator, a crowded train or a long meeting can each be the source. Anyone trying to work out where they caught the flu is usually reconstructing several days of routine contact with colleagues, classmates, relatives and strangers.

The one-to-four-day range gives that reconstruction a practical frame. When one person in a home falls ill, anyone who shared close quarters with them and caught the virus would usually develop symptoms within the next few days. The range also works backward. Someone who feels sick on a Thursday most likely picked up the virus between the previous Sunday and that Wednesday.

The range describes a typical pattern. It is not a countdown that applies identically to every person, and the day symptoms begin can differ from one member of a household to the next.

Incubation and contagiousness run on separate clocks. Adults can begin passing flu to others about one day before their own symptoms appear. The final stretch of incubation can carry a risk for the people nearby, even while the infected person feels well. That overlap helps explain how the flu can move through a family or workplace before anyone has stayed home sick.

Yes. Adults with seasonal flu can begin spreading the virus about one day before they notice any symptoms. That means a person can pass influenza to others while still feeling well.

This pre-symptom window is one reason flu moves so easily through offices, classrooms and homes. A person in this stage has no fever, no aches and no reason to think they are sick. They go to work, ride public transit, eat with family and sit in meetings as usual.

Nothing about that day signals a need for caution. Someone who is coughing knows to stay home or cover their mouth. Someone who feels fine does not. The virus can reach other people before any of the usual precautions begin.

The window also changes how to think about exposure. When someone comes down with the flu, the people they spent time with in the day before their symptoms appeared may already have been exposed. A coworker at the next desk, a partner at home or a friend at a shared meal all fall into that group.

Those contacts will not know right away whether they caught the virus. Flu takes time to incubate after exposure. Any illness from that contact would surface over the following days rather than immediately.

This early stage is distinct from infections that never produce symptoms at all. Here, symptoms do arrive. The contagious period simply starts a little ahead of them, and it continues once the illness is underway.

For households and workplaces, the gap is practical. Waiting until someone looks sick before taking care around them leaves that first day uncovered. Household members of a person who has just fallen ill can watch for their own symptoms in the days that follow. People who know they have been near someone with the flu can stay alert to the first signs, such as a fever. Staying home as soon as those signs appear limits how far the virus travels from there.

People with the flu are most contagious during the first three to four days after their illness begins. That short stretch is when someone sick with influenza is most likely to pass the virus to the people around them.

The count starts with the first symptoms, not the day of exposure. Flu often arrives abruptly. Fever, chills, muscle aches, headache, a dry cough or heavy fatigue can appear over a matter of hours. The day those signs show up is day one of the peak window.

The timing reflects how the virus behaves in the body. Influenza replicates in the cells lining the nose, throat and airways. In the opening days of illness, a sick person's respiratory secretions carry the largest amounts of virus they will produce.

The peak also lines up with the stage when symptoms tend to be at their worst. Fever is usually present, and coughing and sneezing come often. Each cough or sneeze sends virus-carrying droplets into the air around the sick person. Talking releases them too.

That overlap has a practical meaning. The days when a person feels sickest are largely the same days when they are most likely to infect a coworker, a classmate or a family member. Keeping a sick person out of offices, classrooms and public transit during this stretch removes them from shared spaces on the highest-risk days.

Households face the hardest version of the problem. A sick person in a shared home is in close contact with others throughout the peak. They often use the same rooms, the same kitchen and the same bathroom. Where space allows, the sick person can sleep in a separate room and use a separate bathroom during these first days.

The exact length of the peak varies from person to person. Some people cannot say precisely when their illness began, especially if symptoms built up slowly. Counting from the first fever or the first clear sign of illness gives a practical starting point for tracking the window.

The end of the peak is not the end of contagiousness. The amount of virus a person sheds tends to decline after the first few days. It does not drop to zero overnight. Many people can still infect others after the peak has passed.

Most healthy adults can pass the flu to other people for roughly five to seven days after their symptoms begin. That range describes a typical course of illness. It is not a fixed countdown that applies to every person who catches the virus.

The count starts with the first symptom rather than the moment of exposure. Few people know exactly when they picked up the virus. The first day of fever, body aches or chills is much easier to identify. That makes symptom onset the practical starting point for tracking contagiousness.

The risk is not spread evenly across those days. People are most contagious during the first three to four days of illness. The later days of the five-to-seven-day range can still carry some risk of transmission, but they fall after that peak.

The range also leaves out the period before symptoms appear. Adults can start spreading flu about one day before they feel sick. That means the contagious stretch for a healthy adult opens a day before the five-to-seven-day count begins.

Feeling better and no longer being contagious are separate milestones. Energy can return and aches can ease while an adult is still inside the five-to-seven-day window. The CDC's stay-home guidance rests on a measurable sign instead of on how a person feels. It advises staying home until a person has been fever-free for at least 24 hours without fever-reducing medicine.

The healthy-adult qualifier carries weight. Children and people with weakened immune systems can shed flu virus for longer than a week. The five-to-seven-day range does not describe their illness.

Symptoms can also outlast the range. A cough or fatigue may hang on for a week or more after the fever breaks. Those lingering symptoms do not necessarily mean an adult is still spreading the virus at the level seen in the first days of illness.

Children with the flu can shed the virus for longer than a week. That can outlast the contagious period of a typical healthy adult who caught the same infection.

Shedding means a sick child is still releasing virus that can infect someone else. A longer shedding period gives the flu more days to move from that child to the people around them. No fixed end date applies to every child. The contagious period for any one young patient cannot be predicted in advance.

Home is where the longer timeline shows up first. Parents, siblings and grandparents share bathrooms, meals and living space with a sick child. Young children also need hands-on care, from feeding to comforting. That care keeps adults within close range of the child for much of the day.

The longer window also affects family plans. A child can look well again while still shedding virus. Visits with grandparents, a newborn sibling or a relative with a weakened immune system carry more risk during that stretch.

School and daycare return follows the same CDC threshold used for workplaces. A child can go back after at least 24 hours without a fever, with no fever-reducing medicine involved. The guidance applies to children the same way it applies to adults.

Clearing that threshold marks the point to return to class. It does not show that a child has stopped shedding virus. A child back at school may still be contagious for part of that longer-than-a-week window. Classmates and teachers share rooms with that child for hours each day.

People with weakened immune systems can shed flu virus for longer than a week. In people who are severely immunocompromised, that shedding can continue for weeks or even months.

Shedding means the body is still releasing virus that can reach other people. A healthy immune system usually brings an influenza infection under control within a shorter and more predictable stretch. A weakened immune system can let the contagious window run well past the typical range for healthy adults.

The term "weakened immune system" covers a broad group. It includes people whose immune defenses are reduced by an illness. It also includes people whose defenses are lowered by medical treatment. Severely immunocompromised patients sit at the far end of that spectrum. They are the group in which shedding can last the longest.

That range does not set a fixed end date for any one patient. Two people with weakened immunity can follow very different timelines. Each person's contagious period depends on their own condition and care. A clinician who knows the case can advise on how long to keep apart from others.

The longer window carries the most weight for the people around the patient. Household members and caregivers can face exposure for far longer than they would with a healthy adult who has the flu. The same applies to anyone who shares a care facility with the patient.

The CDC advises people with the flu to stay home until they have been fever-free for at least 24 hours without the help of fever-reducing medicine. That threshold is the benchmark for deciding when to go back to work after the flu.

The guidance applies to schools as well as workplaces. The same 24-hour benchmark covers a child heading back to class and an adult heading back to an office. Families, teachers and employers can all work from one standard.

The medication condition carries as much weight as the time limit. Acetaminophen and ibuprofen lower body temperature. They can make a sick person feel better and produce a normal thermometer reading while the infection is still active. A normal reading taken a few hours after a dose reflects the medicine, not recovery.

In practice, the 24-hour count starts only when a fever stays down on its own. A normal afternoon reading after a morning dose of ibuprofen does not count. The clock begins once the medicine has worn off and the temperature remains normal without another dose. If the fever comes back, the person has not yet gone a full day without one, and the count starts again.

The phrase "at least" matters as well. The 24-hour mark is a minimum, not a deadline. Nothing in the guidance requires a person to return the moment the threshold passes. Someone who still feels too weak to work can stay home longer.

Fever works as a marker because it is concrete. It is a sign that the immune system is still responding to an infection. Anyone can check it at home with an ordinary thermometer. That makes it a more consistent test than judging whether a person looks or sounds well enough to be around others.

For workplaces and classrooms, the rule turns a judgment call into one measurable condition. That condition is a full day without fever and without the medicine that can hide it.

Flu spreads mainly through droplets that an infected person releases when they cough, sneeze or talk. Contaminated surfaces can also pass the virus along, but they play a smaller role than droplets moving directly from one person to another.

These droplets come from the respiratory tract, where influenza viruses multiply. A cough or sneeze expels them with force. Ordinary conversation releases them too. A sick person does not need to be coughing hard to put virus into the air around them.

Infection begins when those droplets land in the mouth or nose of someone nearby. Some can also be breathed in. Influenza infects the nose, throat and lungs. The face is the entry point the virus needs to reach.

Close and prolonged contact raises the chance of transmission. Shared offices, classrooms, public transit and family homes keep people within range of one another's coughs and conversations for hours at a time. In a poorly ventilated room, respiratory particles linger longer than they would outdoors or in a space with fresh air moving through it.

The surface route depends on hands. An infected person coughs into a palm or sneezes near a desk. Virus then ends up on a doorknob, phone, keyboard, light switch or shared utensil. Someone else touches that object and then touches their mouth, nose or eyes. The virus reaches the same entry points a droplet would, by a slower and less direct path.

How long flu virus remains infectious on an object depends on the material and the surrounding conditions, including temperature and humidity. Hard, nonporous surfaces such as steel and plastic generally hold infectious virus longer than fabric or paper.

Influenza is an enveloped virus. A fatty outer layer surrounds it. Soap and common household disinfectants break that layer down, which inactivates the virus.

Droplets carry most of the risk. The people most likely to catch the flu are those who share air and space with a sick person. Surfaces add a secondary path, one that runs through hands rather than through the air.

Some people infected with influenza never develop symptoms, yet they can still pass the virus to others. An infection like this carries no warning sign. Neither the infected person nor the people nearby know to take precautions.

This differs from the short window of spread just before symptoms appear. A person in that window will soon feel sick. An asymptomatic infection never reaches the sick stage at all. The person can carry and spread the virus without ever learning they had the flu.

Ordinary speech is one way this happens. Flu travels mainly in droplets. Talking releases them just as coughing and sneezing do. A person with no cough and no sneeze can still pass the virus on during a conversation, a meeting or a shared meal.

Asymptomatic spread also slips past the usual signals people use to decide whether to stay home. The CDC's guidance for returning to work or school centers on fever. Someone who never runs a fever has no trigger to stay home under that rule. They can carry on with their normal routine at the office, on public transit and at home with family.

Without symptoms, these infections also have no visible start or finish. Nothing tells the person when they began carrying the virus or when they stopped. That leaves no practical way for them to time their own precautions.

The protective habits that matter here are the ones that do not depend on how anyone feels. An annual flu vaccine lowers the chance of being infected at all. Regular handwashing and good ventilation in shared indoor spaces also work without anyone needing to notice a symptom first.

Masking when ill offers less help in this case. A person with no symptoms has no reason to put on a mask.

In a household where one person already has the flu, those everyday steps also protect relatives who feel fine but may be carrying the virus without knowing it.

Antiviral drugs such as oseltamivir, sold under the brand name Tamiflu, can shorten a bout of flu. They work best when a patient starts them within 48 hours of the first symptoms.

These drugs act against the influenza virus itself. That sets them apart from over-the-counter remedies that only ease aches, fever or congestion.

The 48-hour window is counted from the onset of symptoms, not from a diagnosis or a doctor's appointment. Someone who wakes up with a fever and body aches has started the clock that morning. Waiting several days to see whether the illness passes on its own uses up the period in which the medicine works best. Once symptoms have run for two full days, that period is over.

Anyone who suspects the flu and wants to consider an antiviral should contact a doctor, nurse or other clinician early in the illness. A call in the first day of symptoms leaves time to decide on treatment inside the window. The clinician can then judge whether an antiviral fits that patient's case.

Shortening an illness is not the same as switching off contagiousness. Oseltamivir can make a flu episode shorter. It does not end a person's ability to spread the virus at a fixed point.

Starting a course of Tamiflu does not mean a patient stops being contagious after a set number of doses or days. That holds whether the drug was started on the first day of symptoms or the second. No antiviral comes with a built-in date when the risk to others ends.

That distinction matters for anyone deciding when to rejoin coworkers, classmates or family. A patient who feels better sooner on an antiviral may still be carrying the virus. The prescription's start date is not a reliable guide to when it is safe to be around other people.

A cough and tiredness can hang on for a week or more after a flu fever has broken. Those leftover symptoms do not necessarily mean a person is still as contagious as they were in the first days of illness.

Recovery from influenza tends to unfold in two stages. The fever, chills and body aches usually fade first. The cough and the low energy often take longer to clear.

That gap can confuse coworkers and family members. Someone still coughing at their desk may look sick to colleagues, even after the period of heaviest viral spread has passed. The cough on its own is a sign of healing airways rather than a reliable gauge of how much virus remains.

The lingering cough reflects the damage the infection does to the airways. Influenza attacks the cells lining the nose, throat and lungs. After the immune system brings the virus under control, that lining still needs time to repair. Irritated airways can stay sensitive in the meantime. A dry, nagging cough is a common result.

Fatigue follows a similar pattern. The immune response that clears influenza also leaves many people drained. Sleep broken up by fever and coughing fits adds to the deficit. Energy often comes back in stages rather than all at once.

Some changes during the recovery phase call for medical attention. A fever or cough that improves and then returns or worsens can signal a complication. Bacterial pneumonia, sinus infections and ear infections are among the complications that can follow the flu.

Influenza can also make chronic conditions such as asthma and heart disease worse. For someone with asthma, a cough that grows harder to control during recovery is a reason to call a clinician.

Difficulty breathing, persistent chest pain or pressure, and ongoing dizziness or confusion warrant prompt care. Those signs can point to a new problem rather than an ordinary slow recovery.

Without those warning signs, a leftover cough and fatigue generally fade with time as the airways heal and strength returns.

Five habits lower the chance of passing influenza to others during recovery. They are an annual flu vaccine for the people around you, regular handwashing, covering coughs and sneezes, wearing a mask while sick and keeping indoor air moving.

Each habit targets a different link in the chain of transmission. Together they cover the hands, the air and the people a sick person is most likely to be near.

Vaccination is the one step that works before anyone else gets sick. A new flu vaccine is produced each year because the strains in circulation change from season to season. Last season's shot may not match the viruses going around now. Household members and coworkers who are vaccinated are less likely to catch the virus and carry it further. For a family caring for someone with the flu, an up-to-date shot for everyone else adds a practical layer of protection.

Handwashing deals with the virus that ends up on hands after coughing, sneezing or blowing the nose. Soap and water remove it from the skin. An alcohol-based hand sanitizer is a workable substitute when a sink is not nearby. The moments that matter most are after using a tissue, before preparing food and before touching shared items such as remote controls, door handles or phones.

Covering coughs and sneezes keeps droplets out of the air and away from the people nearby. A tissue that goes straight into the trash does the job. The inside of the elbow is the fallback when no tissue is at hand. Coughing into bare hands moves the virus onto everything those hands touch next.

A well-fitting mask adds another barrier. Wearing one while sick reduces the droplets released when talking, coughing or sneezing. It is most useful in shared spaces — a kitchen, a car or the waiting room at a clinic or pharmacy.

Ventilation matters because droplets and smaller particles linger in still indoor air. Opening a window, running an exhaust fan or using an air purifier with a HEPA filter keeps air moving. Moving air dilutes what a sick person breathes out. Small, closed rooms hold the heaviest concentration.

Beyond those five habits, distance inside the home also helps. Where space allows, a sick person can sleep in a separate room and use a separate bathroom. Cups, utensils and towels are best kept to one person until recovery. These steps carry the most weight around people at higher risk of serious flu complications. That group includes infants, older adults, pregnant people and anyone with a chronic health condition.

I've Solved The 72-Hour EV Test Drive Conundrum, And Every Automaker Is Free To Steal My Idea (Except Tesla)

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Because Republicans have fought so hard to spread pro-oil propaganda about electric vehicles, the general public largely has no idea whether an EV would work for them or not. Batteries easily last well over a decade and are recyclable, fast chargers are actually pretty common, and lifecycle emissions are way lower for EVs even if you account for mining, but somehow, objectively true statements are controversial now. New EV sales in the U.S. are also struggling, and automakers are struggling to find buyers. The good news is, I have an actual, viable solution that automakers are free steal borrow.

OK, so we've seen that spending time living with an EV, even temporarily, goes a long way to debunking pro-oil propaganda and can change people's minds about whether or not an EV is right for them. Personally, I think the easiest way to make that happen is for owners who want to see EVs get more popular to lend their cars to as many people as they can, for at least 3 days at a time. Ideally, automakers would offer 120-hour test drives to anyone who wants one, but that would be wildly expensive, so I understand why it's hard to even get a 72-hour loan.

Automakers can't sit back here, though. And I'm not just saying that because EVs are the future (they really are), or because I want my nieces and nephews to grow up breathing clean air (I really do). No, it's because they're already shipping EVs to the U.S., and selling more of them would help both sales and profits, while keeping excess inventory from piling up on dealer lots. So here's the idea I'm offering every automaker, free and clear: introduce incentives that encourage dealers to use EVs as service loaners.

When an auto journalist gives business advice, usually, the best thing any automaker can do is the opposite. And that goes double when that advice is on Jalopnik, a site that still loves a good s—post. I'm also well aware I'm not a serious journalist. But tell me the service loaner isn't the most cost-effective way to get customers into an EV long enough for them to realize how great it is to have a gas station in your garage.

I know we all love conquest sales, but current owners are your most likely future customers. The real key to a future EV sale, though, is the low-pressure, no-commitment, just-try-it-for-fun nature of the service loaner. Even people who actively hate EVs would probably be willing to play with one for three or four days while their car's in the shop if the loaner was free. Customers are less likely to think it's some sort of sales trick, too, if the offer comes from a service manager instead of a salesperson.

Considering we're talking about people who already own a car, I don't know if we'd see large numbers of owners picking up their cars, handing over the keys to the loaner EV, and demanding to trade their car in. But as long as you make a good car, there are no downsides to making a positive impression on your customers. And I bet that program would end up being the most cost-effective sales tool in any automaker's arsenal. Not because they're forcing people to go electric but because EVs do such a great job of selling themselves when they fit a prospective owner's lifestyle.

Not everyone can justify an EV. It's pricier and less convenient if you can't charge at home, and some people do crazy amounts of driving or frequent towing. But if you work for an automaker, you know your EVs make better daily drivers for regular homeowners than your most popular daily drivers, and you know they'd sell better if you could get more butts in seats. Now you have a way to get your most likely customers' butts in those seats for far less than the cost of launching a 120-hour test drive program.

Even better, the anti-EV crowd should be all over this one. If EVs are still as bad as they're convinced they are, then a few days driving an EV should be all anyone needs to disabuse them of the notion that they should go electric. Give the skeptics at your company the opportunity to rack up a win and prove this whole EV thing is a waste of everyone's time and money. I mean, they aren't afraid people will like EVs better if they just spend some time with one, are they? Let's put our money where our mouth is and find out.
